# Starship Cadet: dev notes 3

Read this first in any new session, together with **design-doc.md** and **starship-cadet.html** (build 0.14.0). The design doc is the source of truth for what the game is (update it when a design decision is made); these notes cover the build: what's in it, how the code works, the rules, testing and what's next.

This file replaces DEV_NOTES_2. The repo holds it with design-doc.md, the build, the deck plans in `plans/` and cadet_test.py. Git keeps every earlier version, so files keep stable names (no version suffixes).

**Deck 3 is complete (0.7.0, with the promenade dressed and review fixes) and signed off ("ok for now").** **Decks 6, 1 and 2 are built** (0.7.x, 0.8.x, 0.9.x by three architect sessions, merged as 0.10.0) and are waiting for the user's review. All six decks are now in the game. See TEAM.md and section 6e.

The nine small updates once listed here for the design doc (Chef Brennan, Deck 3's rooms and layout, two meals a day, no freight lift and the Cart bay, the one-floor lounge, the music room, the walk-in lift car, the spine behind glass, Pell on Deck 5) are now in design-doc.md.


## Current build: 0.60.2 (about 30,200 lines; the writers' content is about 10,000 of them)

Two decks, joined by a working lift. Deck 4 (Quarters) plays as before. Deck 5 (Operations) is built and dressed: the Works complex, storage, machine shop, shuttle bay with the shuttle Tern, the door gear and the fan. Pell works there.

| Build | What it added |
| --- | --- |
| 0.5.1 | Step 6c turn 1, Deck 5's shell. `DECKS[5]` straight from the plan. Steel, haul, bay and grate floors; hazard bands at doorways; the landing pad. Inner glass walls (`'glass'`) and service hatches (`'hatch'`). Sealed rooms with tap text. Fixed: a sealed door's tap always walked you to its north side, so doors with the locked room to the north said nothing. |
| 0.5.2 | Turn 2, the lift panel and ride. Point lights padded to `LAMP_SLOTS` on every deck so changing decks doesn't recompile shaders. |
| 0.5.3 | Turn 3, the Works complex dressed: job board, tool wall, benches, lockers, tea corner, washroom, storage aisles, machine shop. Fade groups for storage aisles. |
| 0.5.4 | The lift car as a room you walk into, on both decks (asked for mid-step; replaced turn 2's tap-the-door ride). |
| 0.5.5 | Turn 4, the shuttle bay: shuttle, bay doors, booth, door gear and fan (both turning, `SPINS`). Pell moved to Deck 5. Step 6c done. |

| 0.5.7 | The trunks and ladder redrawn to fit the one row beside the car, and carried through Deck 5's spine so they line up deck to deck. |
| 0.5.8 | The hull no longer follows you: hull walls facing the camera stay low, the far ones stay up. The Walls button cycles Auto, Down and Up for inner walls. Stars: a drifting two-layer sky round the ship, and window stars and nebula sliding aft. |

| 0.15.1 | Pillar 1, workstream 1: ship time and saving (the sim engineer, branch `claude/sim-engineer`). New section 13. The ship day lasts 30 real minutes (`SHIP_RATE` 0.8 ship minutes a second); the game starts on Day 1, a Monday, at 06:00, in Bunk 1 (the tile is still `PLAYER_SPEC.start`, [7, 3]). The light follows the clock: night 21:00–06:00, an hour of dusk and of dawn centred on them. The HUD reads "Day 1, Monday 06:00". The game saves itself (localStorage `starship-cadet-save`) every ship hour, on deck change and when the page is hidden or closed, and resumes on the same deck, tile and facing at the same time. New game button (tap twice). The Night/Day button is a debug control, shown only with `?debug`: Night skips to 21:40, Day sleeps until 07:40. `?nosave` turns saving off; cadet_test.py loads with `?nosave&debug`. A test copy pinning the old clock (1 minute a second from 07:40, the old HUD text, no New game button) is ALL IDENTICAL to 0.14.0 with 0 pixels different; unpinned, only screenshots differ (state, paths, collisions, crew identical). Doors: `doorOpenFor(room, who, t, deck)` opens a locked room to you and the invited crew from 15 minutes before an accepted appointment there to 15 after. They come aboard at 10:00 on `arrive` (10 minutes by the Deck 5 lift, tag `arrive`) and leave at 10:00 on `leave` (tag `leave`); before and after they're `away` (deck 0, so no deck draws them). Templates weight their free time (`GUEST_LEISURE`): a scientist the observatory, a delegate the observation room and lounges, a passenger the Commons; a survivor mostly rests in their cabin (`GUEST_REST`) and skips the gym and the store. `dayReport` judges only whole days aboard, and the habit changes never pick a guest. Inert without `GUESTS`. Tested with four stand-in guests over ten days: `dayReport` clean. |

Event guests (`arrive: null`, with `event` and `stay`) are away until section 18 gives their days through `eventGuestDays(g)` → [arrive, leave] in ship days. | 0.26.16 | Pillar 3, C7 event routines (the sim engineer): how the crew live through a ship-wide event, from section 18's `routines`, `needs` and `moments`. `evDay(d)` reads the rules in force in 10-minute slots through `eventNow` (pure). `doubleShift`: the day shift works to 20:30 with dinner at the station, the night shift to 07:15 (the bridge desks are shared, so the two never overlap). `patrol`: the Watch go round the decks' lift lobbies between spells at their station. `closed`: the bow lounge and the call booths are passed by. `stayIn`: that share of free time in their quarters. `rush`: everyone owed a call goes to the booths when comms come back. `needs`: sleep refills slower, company is missed sooner. The party: `evGathers(d)` turns a moment with `gathering: true` into a gathering at the deck architect's venue (`n|@g:<venue>` from `gatherSpots`, filled in order, held for it). Plans on days with no event are identical to 0.26.15 (14 days compared). | 0.26.17 | Joined up with section 18's 0.34.13 (the sim engineer, merging ship integration 0.41.6 and ship events 0.34.11–0.34.13). The rules come from `eventRoutines(t)` and the calls owed from `callOwed(p, startDay)`; closed rooms are room keys ('3\|L'). **One party:** the all-clear party now reaches the plans only through `eventPlan` (section 18's pool '3\|ev:party', the bow-lounge crowd spots); 0.26.16's own moments→gathering path is gone. What it needed in general stays: a pool of the deck architect's `gather` spots fills in their order (nearest the focus first), not from a favourite seat. An event's host (`eventPlan` entry `host: true`, e.g. Pell at his show) is planned first that day, so their seat is theirs. `doorOpenFor` opens for you when `occasionDoorOpen(deck, room, t)` says so (the ready-room summons, the Captain's table, Calder's office). Event guests use `eventGuestDays(g)`. A survivor's first days are on the infirmary's patient beds (`patient-bed`, the deck architect's 0.38.4) when the deck has them, else the infirmary's places as before. |
| 0.26.18 | Seating by friendship (the sim engineer, agreed with the social engineer). With section 20's `affinityPlan(a, b)` (pure, memoised; guests and the player 0), at a mess table, a lounge seat or a bench a seat within 2 tiles of a friend already planned there (affinity 30+) is taken first, and one beside someone they don't get on with (−30 or less) only if nothing else is free. `occ` bookings are now `[from, to, personId]` (every reader uses the first two), and `occ.who` lists each person's bookings. `seatMates(p, S)` and `seatNear(S, id)` are cached on `schedData()`. Without `affinityPlan`, plans are identical to 0.26.17 (14 days compared). With it (a scratch merge with `claude/social`): seats next to a friend go from about 3% to 19% (83 of 430 on day 1); nobody is below −30 today, so the avoid rule is idle. See "Seating by friendship". |

Section 19's always-true stand-in is removed (it read this name already: Schedule drops the shift, Home's on-shift line). The crew's plans don't include you, so nothing else in sections 10, 13 and 14 changes. Section 17's jobs held for you go to the crew on your days off (the systems engineer, told). |

A work day 07:00–15:30: `tend` at their own bed station (the deck architect's `work:garden` places on Deck 3, one each, spread along the beds; until those exist, the garden's standing spots, held so nobody else stands there), `pick` 10:00–10:45, `carry` 20 minutes at the cold store's shelves (3-cold-1/2), then `tend`. On a harvest morning (section 18's 'harvest') they lead it from their stations for its whole window instead of the pick, and carry after; they're on shift, so `eventPlan` leaves them out of the volunteers. `shiftBlocks` reads it like the galley's, so dayReport and day_check judge their shifts. `gardenCrew(d)` → `[{ id, pick, carry, harvest }]` for the galley's yields. `work:garden` places are kept out of desks and stand-ins (the garden books its own). Whoever is on at 10:00 picks 10:00–10:45 (the harvest's window on a harvest morning); the rest is tending. **No carry block:** the galley takes the pick at its end and posts section 17's gardenCarry job. `gardenCrew(d)` → `[{ id, pick, harvest, lead (rota.team 'senior'), hours }]`. An early or late stint eats at the station when it covers a meal window (the galley rota's rule). A gardener whose spot is taken moves to the next free one (checked at the start of each stint). **Stand-ins keyed by station:** a spare station's stand-in is now drawn with `schedU(strSeed(pl.id), d, 7)` instead of its place in the spare list, so a station added on any deck no longer reshuffles the others; this shifts some stand-in days once (explained difference), and `work:garden` stations stay out of the spare list. With the real gardeners (a scratch merge with 963806a and 28cb0bc): someone picks every day, dayReport clean over a week, each on their own `3-gardenwork-n`. |

Each note is kept two ship days as a cue (`simNotes(kind, since)`, newest last, at most 200), saved as `save.notes`. `flag(key)` / `flagSet(key, value = true)` (`null` clears), saved as `save.flags`; both read back defensively. | 0.26.23 | Pillar 4, wave 1a (the sim engineer): day 0, the arrival start, `introPlan`, the escort's lead and follow. **Day 0** (Sunday) plans like any Sunday (day_check day 0 OK; a guest's day of the year now wraps, so day 0 is the year's last). **The arrival start:** `SHIP_START_ARRIVAL` (day 0, 17:40, Deck 5, on the Tern's ramp [7, 8], facing east) and `newGameStart(arrival)`, which section 21 calls (then `introBegin` or `introSkip`); New game clears the flags and notes; a day-0 save loads. **`introPlan(p, d)`** (section 14) turns section 21's `introBeats(p.id, d)` into blocks, laid over the person's day like an accepted appointment (`introLay`: cut out of what they were doing, lift rides before and after a block on another deck, its place held; a beat on shift counts as work in `dayReport`); plans are made again when `introSig()` changes. **Lead and follow (section 10):** while `escortPlan(p, t)` gives `{ mode, to: { deck, tile } | { deck, room } }`, `whereIs` puts that person on your deck (waiting: on `to`'s deck), stepping out of the lift after you (waiting at a tile or place, already there); leading to another deck heads for the lift and `CrewAgent.escortStep` walks them: lead stops facing you beyond 4 tiles and sets off again within 3, follow keeps 2 tiles, wait stands at `to`. **The reviewer's fixes for 0.26.22:** a note's data is copied as plain JSON (a note that can't be, e.g. carrying a walker or a cycle, is dropped with a warning under ?debug), so the save never stops; `SIM_CUE` / `SIM_RING` are `var`; New game clears flags and notes and sends `simNote('newGame', { arrival })` for anyone with state of their own (and calls section 21's `introNewGame(arrival)`). **`simNote('shiftStart', { late })`** for section 20: on your work days, the first minute from 08:00 you're in a Works room on Deck 5 (`late` = minutes past 08:00), or `{ late: 120, missed: true }` at 10:00; once a day (flag `shiftSent`). A tile beat in `introPlan` takes the place in that room nearest the tile (Calder's mess seat at 18:30 on day 0). **`crewComeTo(p, placeId, onArrive, { until })`** (sections 14 and 10): a live visit to a place on your deck (by lift from elsewhere), `onArrive(agent)` once there, ended at `until` (an hour, never past 22:00) or by the returned function; plans unchanged. Also: `simNote('apptKept' | 'apptMissed')` from `apptTick`; a guest never takes a desk someone works at (their own or the day's stand-in's; it showed on day 0 with Cato Kirby); the galley's queue hold applies only on the plan's deck (a released escort on the wrong deck now leaves by lift). |

On the repro (day 22, scratch merge with ship-events 0.34.24): Pell at his farewell from 18:27, 14 of 19 there, dayReport clean. Plans change on days with a gathering (most days now, with the traditions): explained difference. |

On a rotation Sunday a leaver's day ends at the shuttle bay at 15:00 (`ROTA_OFF`) and an arrival's starts there at 17:40 (`ROTA_ON`). An arrival `replaces` a leaver and takes their bed and desk (`schedDesks` runs without the arrivals, then copies), so nobody else's desk or bunk moves; arrivals are never in the habit changes, and a stand-in is drawn only from people aboard all day. `crewLeaving(p)` / `crewArriving(p)` (for section 20's goodbyes and news) and `crewState(d)` → `{ aboard: [ids], left: [{ id, day }], arrived: [{ id, day }] }`, written as `save.crew` (rebuilt from the roster, never from the save). `dayReport`'s bed check compares times, so a bunk handed over on the day isn't a double. The New game button follows section 21's rule: tests and builds without section 21 start on Monday; a device that has never arrived arrives; one that has gets section 19's `deviceChoose` (arrive, or skip to Monday and `introSkip()`). Also (reviewer 1): a first launch's arrival is now section 21's to start (`newGameStart(true)` from its own load), since section 13 can't see section 21 while loading; and a dinner moved before a gathering ends by the gathering's start (a grab if that's too soon). Without the reserve data (this branch alone) plans are identical to 0.26.24. With it (a scratch merge with 98df62f): day 35 Theo Teague boards at 15:00, Basil Bramwell steps off at 17:40 into his bunk and desk; dayReport clean on days 34–36. |

Crew look up for about two seconds at a jolt, each a beat apart (`feelLook`, in `CrowdPerson.update`, so the person you talk to too). Reduced motion: no shake, and the flickers become soft dips. At an event's gathering on your deck (`FEEL_S.gather`: the all-clear party) with more than 40 people on the deck, the coarse crowd starts at 12 tiles (`CROWD_GATHER`, `CROWD_GATHER2`). The party measured (day 13, 19:42, bow lounge, 64 on Deck 3, desktop 900×760): 291 draw calls at zoom 1.15, crowd triangles 209k to 190k; at zoom 0.55, 134k to 73k. Shots: plans/shots/storm-*.png. |

| 0.27.22 | Tall furniture drawn together (section 4c, `tallCombine` and `tallSync`). Each room's tall pieces keep their own copies of their materials so the room can fade when it hides you; that cost one call per material per room. Now after the build, every room's merged pieces are also copied into one mesh per material (and shadow flag) for the whole deck, in room order, with one more frozen copy of the material (so, as before, it doesn't pulse or wilt with the shared one), and the rooms' own meshes are hidden. A room that is fading or faded (`t.o` below 1) shows its own meshes again, and the deck-wide mesh skips that room's stretch through its geometry groups (`tallSync()`, once a frame after the fades; it only changes anything when a room starts or stops fading). Opaque materials only. Desktop 1280×760 by the lift: Deck 4 widest 545→470, zoom 1.0 251→224; Deck 5 widest 363→299; Deck 3 widest 392→356 (Decks 2 and 3 at middle zooms +3 or 4: the deck-wide meshes are never culled). Phone portrait 390×844: Deck 4 widest (0.7) 454→383 (871 at 0.27.18), usual (1.5) 210; Deck 3 317/221, Deck 2 297/188, Deck 5 272/178. cadet_test: one pixel differs (max 11 levels) in the two steering shots, where two bunk rails meet at exactly the same depth and are now drawn in one call, so the tie goes the other way; a faded room on Decks 3, 4 and 5 is pixel-identical to the old build (scratch probe). Memory: 479→435 geometries. |
| 0.27.23 | The garden view (the lead's job 3, with the galley's working garden 0.29.18-0.29.25; merged claude/galley-food e1390c2). The crops on Deck 3's raised beds and bean trellises grow with the galley's beds (`SHIP.galley.garden.beds`: `[crop, grown, days]`): bare soil just after a pick, small seedlings sitting low, full plants by three quarters grown, and tomatoes, bean pods and bean flowers only from 60% (all there when ripe). How: the `raisedBed` and `trellis` kits take `o.live` (a crop id); then `plantSlots` records each plant in `GARDEN_PLOTS` (reset with the deck build) instead of building it, making the same calls in the same order, so R() is drawn exactly as before (the lounge, mess and promenade are pixel-identical to the old build). Section 8b's `gardenView` draws them as instances, one InstancedMesh per shape and material (12 on Deck 3), culled with the garden as a whole; it looks once a second and only rewrites matrices when a bed's growth changes. The nth bed of a crop on the deck is the galley's nth bed of it: greens 4 and herbs 2 (the raised beds), tomatoes 3, beans 2 of 3 (the trellises). Not shown: the third bean bed and the 3 root-vegetable beds (no place for them in the garden; the deck architect could add some); the grow racks' microgreens and the flower beds stay as they were. The garden dress lines (section 4d, Deck 3) gained `live:`. Draw calls with the garden in view: 186→191, the same whatever the growth. The bean leaves don't fade with their trellis when it hides you (the instances aren't part of the room's fade). Shots: plans/shots/garden-{now,ripe,picked,half}.png. |

| 0.28.6 | Pillar 2, B2, the player's side of the job loop (the player engineer, on ship integration 0.40.4). Take a job on the device (section 17's jobs section, 0.33.9), fetch its parts from main storage on Deck 5 (tap a shelf or the counter: 4 minutes, two parts at most), tap the machine: you walk to its panel place, fit what you carry, look it over the first time (3 minutes, it says what it needs) and repair it for the job's minutes (20 / 45 / 90, +25% a tier); walking away pauses it, keeping what's fitted and the time done; finished, `finishJob` puts the machine right. A job needing more than two parts takes two trips. `SHIP.player.carry` and `.work` are saved. Guarded: without section 17's `myJob` and `finishJob` nothing changes. Fetching uses the crew's storage places (`5-parts-1`..`4`); with a part out of stock that the machine shop can make (not in `NO_MAKE`), a tap in the machine shop makes it at `5-make-1`/`2` (an hour, pausable). A "Your device" notice when a job you could take is posted. Built on ship integration 0.40.5 (which has section 17's jobs and the device's Jobs section). |

Cause: only the middle treadmill is an item with a description (the Deck 3 dressing gives `it` to `k === 1` only), and things with no description aren't tappable, so a tap on treadmill 1 or 3 went through to the floor and you only walked there. Fix in section 15 plus one line in section 11's `tap`: before a floor tap walks, `playerFloorTap(point)` follows the tap's line of sight from the floor up to 1.2 m and, if it passes within `FLOOR_NEAR` (0.5 m) of a usable place's spot (`FLOOR_ACTS`: gym, call-home booths, store shelves; or a public seat, `SIT_TYPES`), uses it as a tap on it would (`playerTap` with a stand-in title, or `sitTap`). It also reaches the far mess tables' and two store shelves' places, which had the same gap. Workouts now cost energy too (`PLAYER_ACTS.gym.cost = { energy: 6 }` over its 30 minutes, any activity can carry a `cost`), so the device shows fitness going up and energy going down while you run. Belt: it scrolls whenever `placeBusy` holds the place, which includes the player's (`PLAYER_PLACE`). player_check: real clicks on all three treadmills (belt or console): reached, pose `run`, belt moving, fitness up, energy down. Sunday's side is the winner of the last tasting within the week (the same as `tallyLast`). **Caches follow the events** (reviewer 1's note): `galleyFresh()` clears the ties, menus, tastings, rota and day events when section 14's `evSig()` changes (as `shipDay` does), and the menus when `SHIP.events.votes` changes. **Birthdays** (the lead's ask): `galleyBirthdays(d)` takes `schedPeople()` aboard that evening (`aboard(p, d)`, not on their leaving day; both typeof-guarded) with `birthdayOf(p)` when it's there, matching section 18's honorees, plus your own. **lateMeal** gains `dish` (the hot dish's name, null for the shelf or a missed meal; the dialogue engineer's ask). **The crossover supper** (tie dish `crossover-...`): the dinner, "and pancakes for the night shift, whose breakfast it is". food_check OK on 0.60.0 and on a trial merge with claude/ship-events 5fa104f (day 10: the tally's dishes and votes 7 to 1, your vote through tallyVote once, 7 to 2, Sunday's side the tally's winner). day_check OK, cadet_test ALL IDENTICAL against 0.60.0. |

| 0.29.31 | On 0.60.1 (merged). **The food queue is real** (the user's report on 0.60.1: too long, and "more people in the queue than there are"). The cause: `galleyJoin`'s "ahead" was everyone whose *plan* was in a queue stop plus everyone the galley had waiting (often the same person twice), and the crew never actually reach the queue spots during their 3-minute stop (40 samples at dinner: 0 standing, 1-4 counted); your turn was 1 minute + 45 s a counted person, and section 15 restarted that clock when you reached the line. Now: `galleyAhead(now)` is the crew really standing at (within 0.8 m of) the queue spots on Deck 3 (section 10's `DECK_CREW`); off Deck 3, each person in a queue stop or waiting, once. **Serving pace** (`GALLEY_SERVE`): 0.6 ship minutes a person (a line of 6 clears in about 3.6), a minute a person in a `galleyBusy` rush (where the user's 5-8 line at peaks forms), and yours 1 minute after the last ahead (under 2 real seconds with nobody ahead). **For section 10's live line** (sim engineer 3: `servLine`, `lineJoin`, `lineSpot`, `lineFront`, `lineLeave`): `galleyServeMins(t)` and `galleyCanServe(t)`; when the line exists, `galleyJoin('player')` joins it and returns its spot, `galleyTurn('player')` is true a serve after you reach the counter, `takePortion` calls `lineLeave`. `GALLEY_QUEUE` is front first now (3-queue-4 by the counter). The numbers over your head and in the texts are gone in sections 15 (player engineer 3, 0.28.30) and 19 (device engineer 3, 0.35.27). food_check: "ahead" always equals the crew standing in the line, the serving pace, your turn in 1.0 ship minutes with nobody ahead, the live line with a stand-in. player_check OK (it failed with a half-minute serve: shorter than its sampling step), day_check OK, cadet_test ALL IDENTICAL against 0.60.1. |
| 0.29.32 | On 0.60.2 (merged). **Fitted to section 10's live line** (sim engineer 3's 0.26.30: `servLine`, `lineJoin`, `lineSpot`, `lineFront`, `lineLeave`; section 15's 0.28.34 walks you up it). `galleyJoin('player')` joins it and returns your spot in section 15's terms (`lineSpot`'s world x/z and radians become deck x/y and degrees); `galleyAhead(now, who)` is `servLine()` minus you on Deck 3 (else the 0.29.31 body count); your turn is a serve (1 ship minute) after you reach the counter; `takePortion` leaves the line. New **`galleyLeave(who)`**: out of the line unserved (section 15 calls it from 0.28.35); `galleyTick` also does it once section 15's `ACT` isn't a queue, so a player who walks away never blocks the counter (section 10 skips 'player' at the front). food_check's queue probe no longer joins the line (it reads `galleyAhead`), and with the live line it checks "ahead" against `servLine()` and leaves your own turn to player_check. **Reviewer 1's fixes** (review of 0.29.27-0.29.31): `lateMeal` notes carry `t: m` (when it happened, not the end of a time jump); a fresh galley's catch-up from midnight to now (`GALLEY_QUIET`) posts no late meals (a new game sent 48 at once); the galley's birthdays use section 18's `shipYearDay` (day 0 is no birthday); the tasting plates read their own count, no per-frame object. Tested alone on 0.60.2 (food_check OK: the real tasting table has 25 tasters now that meal traditions are dinner; the catch-up posts 0) and on a trial merge with claude/sim-engineer 0cf991a and claude/player-needs fabc552 (player_check OK: the line holds you, you're served, Leave takes you out; food_check OK). day_check OK, cadet_test ALL IDENTICAL against 0.60.2. Section 16 owns the player's line membership (the lead's ruling): only galleyJoin / galleyLeave / takePortion call lineJoin / lineLeave for the player. |

| 0.33.16 | Answers 1 and 8 (the systems engineer). **Jobs left for you:** the crew take urgent jobs, overhauls and everything above your tier at once; a small job at your tier waits `CREW_LEAVE` (180) minutes *of the day shift* (08:00–17:00) after it's found, so one found in the evening waits for your morning (`leaveEnd(j)`, `DAY_SHIFT`). machine_check reports "jobs left for the player": 3.7 a day over 14 days (target 3 to 6), plus about one daytime food run a day in play. **The cart run as your job:** a food run that isn't urgent, set off on the day shift, waits an hour on the board for you; take it from the device ("Take a hover cart from the Cart bay…"), finish it with `finishJob`. One you leave goes to the crew at the next hour; urgent and night runs go to the crew at once; one nobody takes in 4 hours goes unseen (`CART_GONE`). The galley's books land the meals at `arriveAt` whoever pushes. `jobStops(j)` gives the player engineer the stops in order (`cart load unload return`, or `fetch job`). Optional flicker and roll (off under reduced motion, and a Screen effects switch). Phone: a sheet over the bottom two thirds (toasts move above it; swipe up from the bottom edge opens it, a swipe down on the bezel or a tap on the ship closes it). Wide: a side panel on the left. Three phosphors (amber, green, Wren cyan, the default) in a new Settings app; `SHIP.device.settings` saved. Screenshots in plans/shots/device-*.png. |

Deck buttons 1 to 6; tap a room on the plan or in the list, then "Go there" points the goal arrow at it (`setGoal({ deck, room, label })`; on another deck the arrow leads to the lift first). People on the map wait for relationships (the social engineer). |

Loved gifts +10 and a big memory (and "Loves tea" in what you know), liked +6, anything else +2; double on their birthday. Now and then a friend leaves you something (a note in Mail); keepsakes for your shelf. The store, garden and machine shop still have to hand them out (`giftAdd`). |

Strangers where you're known start warmer, favours come more often, jobs can be "asked for you", Calder leaves notes, departments say thank you, and the ship gossips about you on Sundays. `repView()` for the Record's page (words only). |

It's their tap line and an invitation to accept on the device (a Mail if they haven't seen you by 15:00); keeping one counts as time together and a memory. Also fixes: `socialInvite` placed tradition invitations a day late (plans are in absolute minutes), and reloading reputation changed its save's field order. social_check: about one invitation a day over two weeks, each fitting the person's real plan. |

If you're out, sometimes a note on your bunk (and in Mail). Close friends invite you round to their quarters. At most one visit or note a day. |

## Ship time and saving (section 13, the sim engineer, 0.15.x)

Section 13 sits after section 5 (lighting) and before section 6, not at the end: the clock drives the light, and the save has to be read before section 10 builds the first deck. Nothing in it calls `R()`.

- **Time.** `SHIP.time = { day, min }` (day 1 is a Monday; `min` is fractional ship minutes since midnight). `SHIP_RATE = 1080 / 1800` since 0.26.8 (18 ship hours in 30 real minutes; it was 1440 / 1800, a 30-minute day). `shipNow()` gives absolute ship minutes, `weekday(day)` 0 = Monday … 6 = Sunday, `hhmm(min)`, `clockText()` for the HUD, `setShipNow(abs)`. `tickShip(dt)` is the main loop's step: it does nothing while `document.hidden`, updates the HUD each ship minute and saves each ship hour. The ship doesn't run while the game is closed (no catch-up on load).
- **Jumps.** `skipTo(abs)` moves time forward only, calls every function in `SIM_ADVANCE` with `(from, to)` in absolute minutes, then updates the clock and saves. `sleepUntil(min)` skips to the next time `min` comes round (from 22:15 to 06:00 tomorrow; from 05:00 to 06:00 today). `SIM_ADVANCE` is the hook for the schedules (section 14) to advance everyone's state with no drawing; nothing is registered yet. There is no in-game way to sleep yet (a tap on your bunk is the obvious one, for later).
- **Light.** `nightAt(min)` is 0 by day and 1 by night: 1 from 21:30 to 05:30, easing (smoothstep) through dusk 20:30–21:30 and dawn 05:30–06:30, so it is .5 at 21:00 and 06:00. The main loop eases `nightT` toward it exactly as it used to ease toward `nightTarget`, so per-deck mood light (`applyLight`, `light` in a deck definition) is unchanged. On load `nightT` starts at `nightAt()` so a resumed game wakes in the right light.
- **The Night/Day button** is a debug control, hidden unless the URL has `?debug`. By day (`nightAt() <= .5`) it reads Night and skips to 21:40; by night it reads Day and sleeps until 07:40. Both are `skipTo` jumps, so they also exercise `SIM_ADVANCE`. Those are the old button's times, which keeps cadet_test's night shot comparable.
- **Saving.** localStorage key `starship-cadet-save`, JSON `{ v: 1, build, time: { day, min }, player: { deck, tile, facing }, ship: { decks: { n: { open: [door keys] } } }, crew: {} }`. `ship.decks` holds the doors unlocked per deck (`SHIP.decks[n].open`; only decks with any). Written by `saveGame()` every ship hour, at the end of `goToDeck`, on `visibilitychange` (hidden) and `pagehide`, and after any `skipTo`. `loadGame()` returns null (a fresh start) for no save, a `v` it doesn't know, a deck that isn't built, or anything malformed. Every read and write is in try/catch, so blocked or missing storage just means no saving.
- **Starting.** `START` is `{ deck, tile, face }` from the save, or `{ deck: 4 }` fresh. Section 10 builds `DECKS[START.deck]` and makes the player with `{ ...PLAYER_SPEC, deck: START.deck }` at `START.tile || PLAYER_SPEC.start`; a saved tile that's no longer free falls back to the nearest open one. A resumed game says "Welcome back. Day 3, Wednesday 22:15." instead of the wake-up toast. `GAME_BUILD` (section 13) holds the build number written into saves: bump it with the header's build line.
- **New game** (button, tap twice within 4 s): clears the save and unlocked doors, sets Day 1 06:00, rebuilds Deck 4 with `goToDeck`, puts you back on `PLAYER_SPEC.start` facing as at the start, and saves the fresh game.
- **URL flags.** `?nosave` turns saving and loading off; `?debug` shows the Night/Day button. cadet_test.py loads every build with `?nosave&debug` (builds before 0.15.1 ignore both), and deck_check.py uses the same loader.
- **Hooks in shared code (all small):** the HUD's clock text and the New game button (HTML); `nightTarget` gone from section 5; `updateClock` and the Night button in section 11 call into section 13; `goToDeck` ends with `saveGame()`; section 10 builds `START.deck` and places the player from `START`; the start toast picks welcome back on a resume; the main loop calls `tickShip(dt)` and eases toward `nightAt()`.
- **Testing 0.15.1.** cadet_test against 0.14.0 differs on purpose (the dawn light at 06:00, the clock text, the New game button). To prove nothing else changed, make a test copy that pins the old clock: `SHIP_RATE = 1`, `SHIP_START.min = 7 * 60 + 40`, `clockText` returning `'Ship time ' + hhmm(...)`, the HUD's first text back to "Ship time 07:40", and the New game button `display:none`. That copy is ALL IDENTICAL to 0.14.0, 0 pixels different. Saving was tested with an ad-hoc script: hourly and deck-change saves, a reload resuming deck, tile, facing and time, unknown `v`, junk and unbuilt-deck saves ignored, storage that throws, `?nosave` neither reading nor writing, New game, the hidden-tab pause, `sleepUntil` and the `SIM_ADVANCE` call.

## Schedules (section 14, the sim engineer, 0.21.x)

Section 14 sits right after section 13. Nothing in it calls `R()` or draws anything; every choice comes from `schedU(seed, day, k)`, a hash, so the same crew and places always give the same day, and adding a new decision (a new `k`) doesn't shift the others.

- **Interface.** `planDay(person, day)` gives `[{ from, to, act, deck, type, placeId, fromDeck?, tag? }, ...]`, back to back over the whole day, in absolute ship minutes (`shipNow()`'s units). `whereIs(person, t)` gives `{ deck, act, type, placeId, moving, fromDeck, until, tag }`. `moving` is true only during a lift ride (`act: 'commute'`, `deck` is the deck you're going to, `fromDeck` the one you left). `peopleOn(deck, t)` lists everyone there. `runDay(day, step, fn)` calls `fn(t, [{ p, w }])` every `step` minutes. `dayReport(day)` runs day_check's checks for one day. `schedPeople()` is everyone (the roster plus `ROSTER_GUESTS`).
- **Acts:** `sleep`, `wash`, `breakfast`, `dinner`, `work`, `leisure`, `gym`, `callhome`, `commute`, `off`. Tags say more: `queue` (at the servery before a meal), `grab` (grab-and-go), `tea` (a tea break, counted as work), `rest` (leisure in your own bunk or cabin), `laundry`, `inspection`, `drill`, `room` (washing in your room when every shower and basin was busy).
- **How a ship day is planned (`shipDay`).** Everyone is planned in one pass, in wake-up order (ties by id). Each person's day is built start to finish, taking places that nobody holds yet (`occ`: place → time ranges). When the showers, the servery or the grab shelf are busy, they wait a few minutes where they are, then fall back (shower → basin → their room; mess seat → lounge seat or bench → standing). The last five days are kept.
    - **Beds** are always the person's own (`person.bed`).
    - **Private places:** a place named after a bed (`4-05-wash`, `2-C-wash`) belongs to whoever sleeps there, and nobody else is offered it.
    - **Work stations** are fixed (`schedDesks`): in rank order, each person takes the next station of their type on their work deck, separately for the day and night shifts. Overflow goes to another `work:*` station on that deck nobody on the shift has, else `placeId: null`.
- **A day's shape (`schedFrame` and `schedBuild`).**
    - **Day shift (08:00–17:00):** wake about 06:15, then a wash and breakfast (06:00–08:00) before work; a tea break mid-shift for `tea-break`; then evening specials, dinner (17:00–20:30), leisure and bed about 22:45.
    - **Night shift (21:00–06:00):** after the shift, breakfast, a wash and about 7 hours' sleep through the day, then an evening like anyone's and back on at 21:00. Coming back after a night off: a normal night's sleep, then a four-hour nap in the afternoon.
    - **Days off:** later waking, then leisure.
    - Wake time always leaves 6 h 50 min after the previous bedtime.
    - No lunch, as the design says: two meals a day.
- **Variety, in layers:**
    1. **The rota.** `rota.offDays` gives the days off. A night-shift person's day belongs to the shift that starts that evening.
    2. **The weekday (`WEEK_SHAPE`).**
        - Monday: briefing, the day shift starts 10 minutes early.
        - Wednesday: a 15:00 drill; anyone at leisure musters at a `lift-wait` place for 15 minutes.
        - Thursday: bunk inspection; juniors in Deck 4 bunks spend 10 minutes at their bunk after washing.
        - Friday and Saturday: bedtimes 30 and 20 minutes later.
        - Sunday: anyone off duty lies in 30 minutes longer.
    3. **Jitter.** Waking 5–25 minutes either way, meal lengths, block lengths.
    4. **Habits.** All 18 in `HABITS`:
        - early-riser, late-sleeper, early-to-bed and night-owl move wake and bed times;
        - skips-breakfast, late-diner and lingers-at-meals change meals;
        - workaholic means 15–30 minutes early and late at work;
        - tea-break is a break mid-shift;
        - gym-regular and calls-home are in the specials below;
        - garden-lover, lounge-regular, quiet-corner, common-room and music weight leisure places (`HABIT_LEISURE`);
        - evening-shower moves the shower to before bed;
        - window-seat does nothing yet: no place says it's by a window.
    5. **Need (`schedWants`).** The gym, a call home and the weekly laundry (on the first day off). A habit's pull is raised when it hasn't happened in three days.
- **Leisure choice.** `LEISURE` plus the person's habit places. Places on the deck you're on count four times and the place you just left a fifth, so people don't hop decks every hour (about 5 lift rides a day each). `[bedDeck, 'rest']` is time in your own bunk or cabin. Juniors get Deck 4's common room; officers and up get Deck 1's observation room (`common-room` on Deck 1).
- **Stand-in data.** `STUB_ROSTER` (12 people) and `stubPlaces(n)` are used only where the real ones are missing: `ROSTER` from section 9, and each deck's `places` (a deck with none gets stub places named in the plan's scheme, with ids like `3-mess-seat-1`). Once the real roster and Deck 2's places are merged, the stubs go unused; delete them then.
- **Private places (0.21.2).** `owner` maps a place to the beds it belongs to. A place belongs to a bed when it's named after the bed (`4-05-wash`), or when it's in the same room (`room`) as one or more bed places: an en-suite, a desk or an armchair. Only the owners are offered it. A place with one owner never needs checking; a place shared by bunk-room mates is still checked for overlaps. Roster entries with `player: true`, or with the player's bed, are left out of `schedPeople()`.
- **Going to sleep (0.21.2, section 13).** `offerSleep(worldPos)` is called by the item tap in section 11 (one-line hook) before the item's own toast. If the tapped item is within 1.2 m of the player's bed place (`PLAYER_BED = '4-B1-2'` in `DECKS[4].places`, or tile [7, 1] while there are no places), it handles the tap:
    - From 20:00 to 05:30 (`bedtime()`) it opens the `#sleepAsk` dialog (styled like the lift panel). Sleep fades `#fade` out, calls `sleepUntil(WAKE_AT)` (06:00), fades back in and says "Good morning". Not yet or Escape closes it.
    - At other times it says it's too early.
    Note: Bunk 1's item text calls the bunk at [9, 1] "Your bunk", while the places put 4-B1-2 at [7, 1], with Tomas (4-B1-1). Sleep follows the places.
- **Sleeping.** `SIM_ADVANCE` gets a function that plans the day you skip into. There's nothing else to advance: `whereIs` is worked out from the plan, not stepped.
- **Numbers (real roster and the places pushed so far, Deck 2 on stubs):**
    - Over 28 days: nobody under 6 hours' own-bed sleep (counted from 18:00 the evening before), everyone eats twice and works their whole shift, nothing double-booked, no gaps, and each day joins the next exactly.
    - Unplaced (standing) moments: the Wednesday drill (Deck 1 has 4 `lift-wait` places), and a few when someone lingers where their place is wanted.
    - Peak headcounts per deck: Deck 3 about 64–73 at 17:45–18:20 (dinner plus the Commons in the evening), Deck 4 about 60 at 06:45, Deck 2 about 20, Deck 5 about 13, Deck 1 about 10, Deck 6 3.
    - Speed: planning a day takes about 11 ms; `runDay` at 1-minute steps about 30 ms; `dayReport` about 17 ms.
- **Testing it:** a scratch copy of the build with section 9's `ROSTER` block and the architects' `places` lists pasted in (not committed), then `dayReport(d)` for many days, the seams between days, and determinism (the same plans after clearing the cache and planning other days first).
## Places on Decks 4, 5 and 6 (0.17.x, the Deck 6 architect)
Pillar 1, workstream 3a; the format is in plans/pillar1-plan.md, "Places". Each of `DECKS[4]`, `DECKS[5]` and `DECKS[6]` has a `places` list, written as plain data just before `lockedRoom` (Deck 4) or `markings` (Decks 5 and 6).
- **Where they sit.** On the furniture that's there: the tile of a seat, bed, bench or stall, or the free tile in front of a bench, console or machine for someone standing at it. Seats and beds are reached from a tile beside them. Nothing was moved to make room for them.
- **`face`** is the way the person faces there: away from a seat's back, towards a console. For beds it points at the side you get in from.
- **Deck 4 bunks (0.17.1, Sketch 3):** each bunk room is 5 × 4 with three bunk beds, so berths 1–2 are the first bunk (lower, upper), 3–4 the second and 5–6 the third, both berths on the bunk's tile. The rooms number 1–8 as the game names them (a = Bunk 1 … h = Bunk 8, the night shift).
- **Wash pods:** every cabin's pod is one `shower` place (`4-xx-wash`); it stands for the pod's toilet and basin too.
- **The check:** deck_check.py checks every place: unique ids ship-wide, the agreed types, poses and faces, the room against the map, and that crew can reach it from the lift. Crew have keys, so 'closed' doors pass; hatches and walls don't.
- **Fixed in 0.17.1:** Deck 4 slept only 32 juniors in 0.17.0. Sketch 3 of plans/deck4-plan.html widened the bunk rooms to 5 tiles, so the bunk template fits three bunks: 48 berths.
- **`floorSeeds` (0.17.1, shared, inert unless a deck sets it):** the floor painter draws random numbers tile by tile, row by row, so a layout change shifts every later number: the floor grain and every piece of furniture and crew choice after it. `floorSeeds: { col, rows }` sets the random sequence to `rows[j]` when the painter reaches column `col` of row `j`. Deck 4 uses column 21 with the values 0.17.0 had there, so everything east of the junior wing, and everything built after the floor, is unchanged. If the junior wing changes shape again west of column 21, keep the values; if anything at column 21 or east of it changes, the values need measuring again (or dropping, if pixel identity isn't wanted).
- **0.17.1 proof (Deck 4, 0.17.0 against 0.17.1):** map cells changed only in columns 10–20; wall and door edges up to column 21; blocked tiles up to column 20; items, spots and closed doors in the officers' wing (columns 24 and on) identical; room tiles changed only in rooms a–h and l; 326 of 3000 collision probes changed, all at column 21 or west; all 9 test routes wholly in the officers' wing and east lobby identical. An officers'-wing view with crew hidden differs only where the shrunk scrubbers room shows over the wall at the top edge. The player still starts at (7,3) in Bunk 1. Crew start in the same places, but drift apart within seconds: the placeholder wander shares one random sequence between all crew, and the bunk rooms offer different spots, so cadet_test differs throughout (as expected; the new baseline needs the lead's sign-off).
- **Service hatches and still floors (0.30.1), for every architect building a utility room:** give the room a floor with `still: true` (no random numbers, so the rest of the deck is unchanged), make its doorway `'hatch'` in `doors`, and add the key to the deck's `service: [...]` list. The player and Works crew can then pass; the hatch's sealed/locked tap text is dropped. Crew paths use it once crewPath passes the person as findPath's `who` (the sim engineer's side).
- **Machines (0.30.2 on):** `at` is the machine's tile in front of its panel, `face` is the way its front faces (toward the panel), `panel` is where a Works tech stands; the place `<id>-svc` (type `work:works`, pose `work`) on the panel faces the machine. Utility-room furniture goes last in a deck's dress() and never calls R(), so nothing else on the deck moves.
- **Pillar 2, A5a (0.30.x): the utility rooms on Decks 4, 5 and 6, and the gym treadmill.** Proposed in a "Utility rooms" section on each of plans/deck4-, deck5- and deck6-plan.html (29 machines: Deck 4 has 7, Deck 5 has 18, Deck 6 has 4). Every room keeps its size and position. Each machine has an id, a kind, a footprint, a panel tile and a `work:works` place on it. Waiting for the lead's OK before building. Before writing the sections I checked every layout: each panel tile is free floor, faces its machine and can be reached from the hatch. Once built, the `machines` lists in the game are the source of truth.

## Crew on the deck (section 10, the sim engineer, 0.22.x)

- **Agents.** `DECK_CREW` holds a `CrewAgent` for everyone `whereIs` puts on the deck you're on. Each frame (`updateDeckCrew`, called from the main loop before `crowd.update`):
    - An agent asks `whereIs` where they should be: a place id; null (stand where you are); or, when their schedule has them on another deck, `'@lift'` (walk into the lift car and vanish).
    - When the goal changes they `retarget`: up from the seat to its walk-to tile, then a path (`crewPath`) to the new place's walk-to tile, then a short glide onto the exact spot, and the place's pose (`lie` for any bed).
    - Every quarter second, anyone due on the deck who isn't there arrives: out of the lift car when their ride ends, or straight at their place if they were missed.
- **Where a place is (`placeSpot`).** The walk-to tile is the place's `walk`, its own tile if open, or the open neighbour nearest the spot. The spot is `xy`, or the tile centre. Beds without `xy` use the middle of the nearest bunk, bed or futon item, since a bed covers two tiles. Upper berths (even-numbered `4-B<n>-<berth>`) lie at 1.6 m; everything else uses the crowd's default heights.
- **Arriving on a deck (`deckCrewFill`).** Called from `goToDeck`, at load, and after any time skip (`SIM_ADVANCE`). Everyone is placed mid-activity. If their current activity started less than a walk ago, they're partway along the path from where they were (their previous place on this deck, or the lift), and keep walking; otherwise they're at their place. People still riding the lift step out when the ride ends.
- **Locked doors.** `crewPath` is `findPath` with the 'closed' doors into the agent's rooms opened for the call: the room of their own bed and the room of the place they're going to. `crewNearDoor(d)` opens a door for any agent walking near it; for a 'closed' door, only for agents whose rooms it leads to. Hook: two lines in the main loop's door code. The player still can't pass.
- **Crew and you.** Crew don't block each other (they never share a place, so they only cross in passing). Standing crew push you out when you steer (`Walker.update`), and `occupiedBy` keeps your paths off their tiles. Tap a crew member (`tapCrew`, by screen distance to their body) and they stop for 6 seconds, facing you if they're on their feet; the toast gives their name, role and bio. The Crew button hides the crowd (`crowd.visible`); they carry on unseen.
- **Stuck.** `stuck` counts ship minutes an agent meant to move but didn't; `stuckMax` keeps the worst. `noPath` counts times there was no way to a place (the agent is then simply put there).
- **The old walkers.** `CREW` (section 9) stays as data; `crew` is an empty array for the code that still loops over it. The rig, the player's `Walker` and its R() calls are unchanged, so with crew off nothing else moves.
- **Overflow stations (0.22.4).** `schedDesks`' second pass (anyone whose own type of station has run out) takes another free `work:*` station on the work deck, but never one in a locked room (`schedRoomLocked(n, room)`: a room whose every door is 'closed') or a private one (places in a bedroom are already owned).
- **Draw calls (0.22.1, by the lift at 06:00):** Deck 4 with 52 crew, 319 (0.22.0 with its five walkers: 346); Deck 3 at 18:00 with 63 crew, about 307–325.
- **Full rigs (0.22.2).** Every quarter second `crewDetail` gives the `FULL_MAX` (3) story crew nearest you, within `FULL_NEAR` (4.5) tiles, a `FullPerson`, and sends them back to the crowd beyond `FULL_FAR` (6). A `FullPerson` is a baked `makeCharacter` rig in the scene, using `CrowdPerson.prototype.set` and `.update`, so it's posed exactly like the crowd (sit, lie, work, eating). That relies on `CrowdPerson`'s field names; if section 8b changes them, change `FullPerson` too. Each full rig costs about 30 draw calls. It has its own random stream (from the person's id), and its geometry is disposed when it goes back to the crowd.
- **Walk-to tiles (0.22.2).** For a place on a blocked tile (a seat or bed) with no `walk`, the candidates are the open neighbours in the place's own room that aren't behind a wall, nearest the spot first. The first one `crewPath` can reach from the lift wins.
- **day_check.py (0.22.2).** `python3 day_check.py BUILD.html [DAY]`, about 4 minutes. It loads with `?nosave&debug` and switches the game's clock tick off, then:
    1. `dayReport(DAY)`: sleep, meals, shifts, double-booking and gaps, plus the peak headcounts.
    2. The plans come out the same after clearing the cache and planning other days first.
    3. Each deck runs a whole ship day of crew logic in 0.05 s steps (`updateDeckCrew` only, no drawing), checking: nobody stuck more than 10 ship minutes; nobody settled in a place someone else holds; nobody lying in a bed that isn't theirs; nobody with no way to their place; nobody due on the deck missing. Every ship hour it draws one frame by the lift and one over the crowd, and the peak must stay under `BUDGET` (450).
    4. Deck 4 runs twice without drawing and must end identically.
- **day_check numbers.**
    - Day 1 (Monday):
        - Crew per deck at most 7 / 19 / 69 / 62 / 12 / 3.
        - Draw-call peaks 370 / 373 / 415 / 411 / 377 / 321 (Deck 3's at 07:00 by the lift, Deck 4's at 07:00).
        - Worst stuck 0.0 ship minutes.
    - The whole first week (days 1–7): OK. The draw-call peak over the week is 425 (Deck 4, Friday).
- **Fixes found by day_check (0.22.2):**
    - Someone told to stand anywhere while still sliding into a seat set off from the seat's furniture tile into the next room. Agents now always set off from the place's walk-to tile.
    - Someone at an overflow desk in the locked ready room couldn't get out. Agents can always pass the doors of the room they're in.

## Every room in use (sections 14 and 10, the sim engineer, 0.26.x)

Pillar 2, workstream A1 (plans/pillar2-plan.md).

- **Rooms in use.** `roomsInUse(d0, days)` gives, per deck and place type, how many places there are, how many were used, the uses, hours and different people over those days. day_check prints a week's summary from its day: the types nobody used and those fewer than 3 people used.
    - Before A1: 37 of 57 types used.
    - 0.26.1: 54 of 57 used. Still unused:
        - Deck 2's `work:command` desk, which is in a locked room;
        - the store's `work:store` counter, until someone staffs it (asked the lead);
        - Deck 6's `lift-wait` places, because the engine crew leave Deck 6 only at the end of a shift, and the lift wait never cuts into work.
- **New activities (0.26.1).**
    - **Toilet** (`wash` with tag `toilet`, then `hands` at a basin, with no waiting for the basin): half the crew in the morning, most mid-shift, 12% of leisure blocks, 40% before bed. On the deck they're on; their own en-suite if they're on their bed deck.
    - **Tea break:** the habit, or 35% of work days, 15 minutes at a `lounge-seat` or `bench` on the work deck.
    - **Shopping:** 10% of work days, 45% of days off. Browse a `shop` place for 10–22 minutes, then pay at a till for 2 minutes. A till is any place with `anim: 'pay'`, listed under the pseudo-type `pay` so browsers don't stop at it.
    - **Waiting for the lift:** a minute at a free `lift-wait` place, taken off the end of the activity before a ride (leisure, meals, the gym, calls, off), so no timing moves.
    - **Works crew wash up** after the shift: a basin, or 30% of the time a shower, on the work deck.
    - **Lounges:** Deck 1's `lounge-seat` for officers; Deck 2's `lounge-seat` and `bench` for officers and anyone who works on Deck 2; Deck 4's `bench`.
    - **The gym** goes after dinner half the time, to spread the 17:00 rush.
    - **Night shift** always showers after the night's work.
    - Shift breaks (tags `tea`, `toilet`, `hands`) and galley staff meals (`staff`) count as work in `dayReport`.
- **Stand-ins (`occ.rot`).** `schedData().spare` lists the stations nobody on the day shift holds, outside locked rooms. Each day, `shipDay` picks one person from each such station's department (day shift, working that day, not galley) to work there all shift; their own station is free that day. Today: Deck 1's `work:survey` and `work:works` stations, and Deck 5's `work:watch`.
- **Needs (0.26.1).** `NEEDS = ['hunger', 'energy', 'hygiene', 'fitness', 'social']`, 0–100.
    - **`crewNeeds(p, t)`** reads them off the person's plans over the week before t. Each starts at 70 a week earlier.
        - Hunger: −5.5 an hour; a sit-down meal sets 100, grab-and-go 50.
        - Energy: +14 an hour asleep, −3.8 awake.
        - Hygiene: −2.1 an hour; a shower sets 100, a basin +15, the gym −10.
        - Fitness: −0.35 an hour; the gym +35 (+15 if under 24 minutes).
        - Social: +15 an hour in company (`SOCIAL_TYPES`, or at work), a call home +35, −2.5 an hour alone.
    - **Steering the plans.** The day's choices use `needsEst(p, d)`, a start-of-day estimate read from the habit-only ("base") choices of the days before. That way planning a day looks back a fixed week and never all the way to day 1.
        - Fitness: 105 − 9 per day since the last base gym visit.
        - Social: 100 − 9 per day since the last base call home.
        - Energy: last night's sleep against 8 hours.
        These steer the gym (base + (100 − fitness)/100 × 0.9), a call home (base + (100 − social)/100 × 0.6), and leisure. Tired people rest twice as much, lonely ones half as much.
    - **Numbers, day 1:** lowest/middle at 23:00 are hunger 71/75, energy 35/45, hygiene 51/88, fitness 3/90 and social 94/99. One person's fitness stays low: of the gym visits her plans want, 2 in 5 happen, because the gym is full or the evening runs out.
    - The ship-day cache keeps 10 days back, for `crewNeeds`.
- **Jobs on the deck (0.26.5).** `whereIs(person, t)` asks section 17's `jobWhere` first. A claimed job's segments (to Deck 5 storage for parts, the lift, the machine's panel place, back to the desk) replace the plan for that time, and the crew walk them like any plan, through service hatches (`hatchPassable`). `planWhere(person, t)` is the plan alone: dayReport, and section 17's `jobRun` (which fits jobs into the plan), must read it. `JOB_WHERE_BUSY` stops `jobWhere` re-entering itself. (0.26.6: `jobRun` reads `planWhere`, so the overlay is always on; `jobsSafe` is gone.) `jobVisits()` counts the places the jobs on the books took the crew to (panels marked), and day_check prints it after its day.
- **Poses on the deck (0.26.4).** `crewPose(agent)` gives the crowd its pose string (8b, the performance engineer: 'base:activity' when seated or lying, the activity alone when standing):
    1. The plan's tag first (`TAG_ACT`): `play` → play, `bar` → drink, `wash` (the dish pit) → wash, `tea` → the place's own pose.
    2. Then sitting about: on a `lounge-seat`, `common-room` or `bench` with no `anim` of its own, read (45%), talk, or (lounge seats only, 20%) drink, seeded per person, place and day.
    3. Then the place itself: `poseOf(place)` (8b), with the place's `anim` or its type's activity, so treadmills run, the rower rows, the bench press lies, booths call, the till pays.
    Mess seats stay plain 'sit' (the crowd eats there). If `CROWD_ANIMS` isn't defined, the plain pose. In the plans, `anim` is the activity (for `whereIs`); a take can set it outright (`o.anim`: the musician's 'play', the bar's 'drink'). The music room's own default is listening.
- **Held things (0.26.7).** `CrewAgent.update` keeps `this.hold` from `whereIs` (`w.hold`), and `heldPose(agent)` appends it to the pose ('walk+cartfull', 'stand+carry') everywhere the pose is sent: `h.set` in `update` and in `crewDetail`'s swap, and while talking. `crewPose` is unchanged: the held thing goes on top of whatever pose it gives.
- **The player's place (0.26.4).** `PLAYER_PLACE` is `playerHolds()` (section 15) each frame. A crew member whose goal is that place stands nearby until you're done, and `placeBusy` counts it as busy.
- **Activities as poses.** A segment carries `anim`: the place's `anim`, or by type (`PLACE_ANIM`: gym `lift`, callhome `call`, shop `browse`, music `play`, laundry `fold`, work:galley `cook`; a standing garden place `water`). `whereIs` returns it. Section 10's `crewPose(spot)` sends it as the pose only when section 8b's `CROWD_ANIMS` lists it; otherwise the place's own pose, so a seated pianist stays seated until 'play' exists. Collision and taps read the place's plain pose.
- **The galley rota hook.** `galleyBlocks(p, d)` calls `galleyShift(p, d)` from section 16 when it exists, for galley staff on day-shift work days. It returns work blocks `[[from, to], ...]` in minutes from midnight. A galley day then goes:
    1. Up 35 minutes before the first block.
    2. Between blocks: free time, and a meal in the mess if its window has 20 minutes free in the gap.
    3. A meal window covered by work: a staff meal at the station (tag `staff`) from 30 to 10 minutes before the window ends.
    4. After the last block, the usual evening.
    `schedFrame` pulls the previous bedtime earlier for an early start. Tested with a stand-in rota (Brennan 04:30–08:10 and 15:30–20:30, hands 05:30–08:30 and 16:00–20:30): 28 days of dayReport clean.
## Ship events (section 18, the events engineer, 0.34.x)

To the player these are **ship traditions** (the user's name, 2026-10-16); the big story ones are "ship-wide events" (Pillar 3). In the code they keep the `SHIP_EVENTS` / `eventsOn` names.

The small, recurring ship traditions (the user: "lots of little holiday-like things, almost like Stardew Valley"). Not the big ship-wide story events (Pillar 3). Section 18 sits after section 17, before section 6.

- **The table, `SHIP_EVENTS`.** Each entry: `id`, `name`, `k` (its own hash key, 320-333: never reuse one; section 14 uses 500-515 and 9100/9200, section 16 301 and 310 up; `k + 100` is the per-person end jitter), `when`, `at: [from, to]` (minutes from midnight), `min` (the shortest stay worth going for), `where`, `who`, `anim`, optional `prio` (the higher wins a clash), optional `jitter` (each person leaves up to that many minutes early) and `early` (birthday people arrive that much early), optional `decor`, and the texts: `from` and `mail` (the evening before), `ask` (the tap line before), `here` (the tap line during), `thanks` (the toast when the player joins). Texts can be functions of the day's event (a birthday names the person).
    - **when:** `{ weekday }` (0 = Monday), `{ every, on }` (days with `d % every === on`), `{ yearDay }` (1-364, `shipYearDay(d) = (d - 1) % 364 + 1`, Day 1 a Monday), or `{ birthday: true }`.
    - **Pillar 4's rhythms (0.34.19, `evDue`):** `{ every: 'week'|'2weeks'|'4weeks', weekday, week? }` (week 1 is the cycle's first, counted from Day 1), `{ every: 'quarter', week, weekday }` / `{ every: 'quarter', quarterDay }`, `{ every: 'year', dayOfYear }` / `{ every: 'year', week, weekday }`, `{ after: 'arrival', days }` / `{ after: 'arrival', weekday, nth? }` (from the day you came aboard), each optionally with `season`, `from`, `until`. **Seasons:** `season(d)`, `seasonOf(d)`: Sowing (days 1-91), Greening (92-182), Harvest (183-273), Lanterns (274-364), repeating. **Ties:** `ties = { galley: { dish, batch, meal }, garden: 'sow'|'tend'|'harvest', decor: id, tally: { kind, choices } }`, read by each owner with `tradTies(kind, d)`.
    - **where,** best first: `{ deck, pool }` (any of schedData's shared keys: `gym`, `music`, `garden-tend`, `mess-seat`...), or `{ deck, room, types, ids?, free?, who? }`: the shared places of those types in the room whose name matches `room` (not in a locked room), optionally narrowed by an id regexp, `free` (nobody's work station), `who: 'officer'` (officers and up only: Deck 1). Places tagged `event: '<id>'` by an architect are taken first, ahead of the rest (section 14's `'n|@<id>'` list when it has them, else section 18's own `'n|ev:<id>'`).
    - **hold:** which `where` entries section 14 keeps for those going during the window (`eventPlaces(e)`, read by the sim engineer's reservation in `shipDay`, 0.26.12): default `[0]`, the first; `[]` holds nothing (the big, casual events: Founding Day, hull lights, the quiet watch). Tagged places are always held.
    - **who(p, d, event):** the chance a free person goes. Free means awake, off duty for the whole window with 15 minutes either side (the shift, galley stints, a night worker's day sleep) and 20 minutes before bed (`eventFree`, from `schedFrame` and `shiftBlocks` only).
    - **anim(p):** what they do there, one of 8b's ACTS (talk, drink, play, water, wash, read), or null for the place's own pose. It goes on the plan as the tag `ev-<id>-<anim>` (`ev-<id>` when null), and section 18 registers those tags in section 10's `TAG_ACT` (`eventTags`, on the first tick).
- **The events now (12):**

  | Event | When | Where | Who |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Running club | Mondays 19:15-20:00 | Deck 3 gym | runners, gym regulars |
  | Observatory night (`observatory-night`) | Saturdays 21:15 for 50-80 minutes | Deck 2: the tagged observatory seats, then the observatory, chart room, science corridor benches | stargazers, 30% of the rest |
  | Quiz night | Thursdays 20:00-21:30 | Deck 3 bow lounge | anyone; bar and lounge regulars most |
  | Works Friday tea | Fridays 18:45-19:30 | Deck 5 tea corner, spare Works places | Works crew (tea, wiping tools) |
  | Music jam | Fridays 19:45-21:15 | Deck 4 music room | musicians play, music lovers listen |
  | Film night | Saturdays 20:00-22:00 | Deck 4 junior common room | juniors |
  | Sunday swap meet | Sundays 10:30-12:30 | Deck 3 promenade and plaza benches, two stalls | shoppers, anyone |
  | Harvest morning | every other Saturday (days 6, 20, 34...) 09:30-11:30 | Deck 3 garden | gardeners, garden lovers |
  | Birthday | the person's birthday (`ROSTER[i].birthday`), 20:15 for 40-55 minutes, them 5 minutes early | Deck 3 mess: the first two long tables (the cake), then any mess seat, the lounge, benches | them, 80% of their department, 70% of bunkmates, 45% of the rest |
  | Hull-lights evening | every 28 days (days 24, 52...) 21:00-22:00 | Deck 3 bow lounge, Deck 1 observation room (officers), promenade | anyone |
  | Founding Day | day 100 of the year, 19:30-21:30 | Deck 3 mess and bow lounge, a cake | nearly everyone |
  | The quiet watch | day 300 of the year, 21:30-22:15 | Deck 1 observation room, Deck 2 observatory, bow lounge | anyone |

- **Traditions as content (Pillar 4, 0.34.20-0.34.21):** tradition designers write `content/traditions/<id>.trad` (grammar and examples: `plans/trad-format.md`); content_gen.py bakes them into section 22's `TRADITIONS` block (`{ id: tradition }`, never hand-edited), parsing each field with `plans/trad_parse.py` (mine), and `tradLoad` turns each into a `SHIP_TRADITIONS` entry at load (rules and texts as data, `TRAD_ERRORS` for anything that doesn't resolve; `TRAD_STANDIN` until section 22's block was merged; gone in 0.34.28).
- **Invitations (0.34.22):** `invite: { host, odds, line }` on a tradition is for section 20's invitations (running, observatory night, quiz, Works tea, film, harvest have one).
- **After each tradition (0.34.21):** `simNote('tradition', { id, day, at, came, you, host, src })` once its window ends (`eventsNoted`, saved in `SHIP.events.noted`).
- **The voice budget (0.34.21):** `evBudget` in `eventsOn`: at most two traditions an evening (from 17:00), never two at once on a deck; birthdays never moved. `EV_DROPPED` (day -> ids left out). Hash keys for new traditions: `k` 434-499 (the traditions engineer gives them out), and their end jitter `k + 100` (534-599).
- **Birthdays.** `birthdayOf(p)` reads `ROSTER[i].birthday` (1-364) when it's there; until the crew designer adds them, a stand-in spread about one a week in id order. Several on one day share one gathering.
- **How it reaches the plans (section 14, the sim engineer's hooks):** `schedData` calls `eventPools(shared)` (section 18 works out the desks itself), which adds pools named `deck|ev:<id>:<n>`. The sim engineer's `gatherings(p, d, F)` turns each `eventPlan(p, d)` entry into a gathering `{ at, dur, where, tag, anim, min }`, and when `eventPlan` is there its own birthday and observatory night switch off (0.34.1 merges their 0.26.9 and adds those lines, the agreed shape of their 0.26.10). `leisureUntil` stops a leisure block at a gathering's start and takes one of its places (within 10 minutes of the start, `by` the end of their free time), else they carry on. So events only ever use leisure time: shifts, meals and sleep never move, and anyone who finds the room full just does something else. People may stay on up to about 20 minutes after the end (the leisure loop's `linger`). `eventPools` and `eventPlan` do nothing until `EV_READY` (a `var` set at the end of section 18), and section 18 clears the plan caches once when it loads, in case anything planned a day before it ran.
- **On the deck (`eventsTick`, main loop, once a ship minute):**
    - **Mail:** an announcement at 18:00 the evening before (`eventsMail`, also on skips through `SIM_ADVANCE`, at most three days' worth), kept in `EV_MAILBOX` (the last 20, for the device's calendar later) and sent through section 15's `mailSend` when it's there: with a toast when it arrives in play, quietly (`quiet: true`) before 06:30 on Day 1 and after a sleep or skip. `SHIP.events.sent` keeps each one to once (saved as `ship.events`).
    - **Decor:** `EV_KIT.cake` (a stand-in on the mess hall's first long table, only without section 16: the galley bakes for `cake: true` events and shows its cake on the servery, 19:30-21:30) and `EV_KIT.stalls` (two trestle stalls at the promenade's east end, against the walls either side of cols 39-40) from 30 minutes before to 15 after. 5 and 8 meshes, no shadows, colours through `col()`, added to `deckRoot` so a deck change takes them away. Not in the collision grid.
    - **Joining:** five ship minutes in one of the event's rooms while it's on: social +12, a toast (`thanks`), and a thank-you in the Mail. Once per event (`SHIP.events.joined`, saved).
    - **Tap line:** `eventLine(p, t)`, read by `tapCrew`: the `ask` line if their plan has them going later today, `here` while they're there, in place of the bio.
- **Numbers (days 1-28, 0.34.1):** quiz 9-21 people, observatory night 4-9 (of 12 places), birthdays 26-37, film 6-10 (of 12), jam 3-5 (of 6), Works tea 7 (full), market 5-7, harvest 6, running club 3-9, hull lights 18, Founding Day 51, the quiet watch 26. Everyone reaches their place on the deck; draw calls at the events (settled frames over the crowd) at most 397. Crowd size barely moves them: a fixed view of the bow lounge reads about 408 with 5 or 22 people in it.
- **Testing:** `events_check.py BUILD.html [DAYS]` (default 28, plus days 100 and 300; about 5 minutes). Good looks like: every event happens when due and never otherwise; at least one person at each; only in its window (plus 25 minutes' lingering) and only at its places; dayReport clean on every day; the same plans twice; on the deck everyone reaches their place, nobody stuck, nobody sharing, draw calls under 450; decor on during, gone after; one Mail per event at 18:00 the evening before; the player joins the quiz; a tap line before it. Ends "EVENTS CHECK OK". It resets `SHIP.galley` before each deck run, because jumping weeks ahead empties the cold store (no carts on paper).

### Ship-wide events (0.34.7, Pillar 3 C6)

The big events (plans/pillar3-plan.md Part 2): a layer over normal life for 2-4 days, never a mission. All in section 18 after the traditions.

- **Format:** `SHIP_EVENTS.<id> = { title, days, earliest, gap?, draft?, phases: [{ id, at: [day, min], clears? }], systems, routines, galley, jobs, news, moments, meetings, address, mail, debrief, threads, traces }`. Day 0 is the warning's day. A phase with `clears` (resolution, afterglow) drops what earlier phases changed.
- **Reading it (other sections):** `eventNow(t?)` gives `{ id, phase, day }` or null; `eventMod(kind, key, t?)` gives the current phase's value, else an earlier phase's back to a `clears`, else null. Kinds: `wear` and `effect` (from `systems`), `routine`, `galley`, `jobs`, or any table on the event. Every reader treats null as "no change".
- **Running it:** `eventAdvance(t)` (from `eventsTick` every ship minute and from `SIM_ADVANCE` on skips, quietly): starts the next event in `EVENT_ORDER` when due (`eventDueDay`: its `earliest` day plus 0-2 by hash, at least `gap` days after the last; never a `draft`), runs each phase's `address` and `mail` as it begins, and at the end scores the threads, adds the band's traces to `SHIP.traces`, logs it in `SHIP.eventLog` and sends the `debrief`.
- **Threads:** `{ id, label, good, bad }`, lower is better (<= good smoothly, >= bad roughly). Other sections add with `eventScore(thread, n)`; the player's part with `eventMine(what, n)`. The result is the bands' average.
- **Saved:** `ship.event`, `ship.eventLog`, `ship.traces`.
- **Stand-ins for other owners:** `captainAddress({ lines, at })` (a plain banner for 20 seconds and a quiet Mail copy, kept in `ADDRESSES`); `MEETING_SCRIPTS`, `meeting(m)` (into `MEETINGS`), `dialogueShow(id)` / `dialogueClose()` (a toast until section 19's `deviceDialogue(script, i)` exists; walking away mails the gist); `upcomingTraditions(from, to)` for the Calendar.
- **The ion storm (0.34.8; switched on in 0.34.12, day 10 in a new game):**
    - **Phases:** warning (day 0, 06:00), onset (1, 14:00), escalation (2, 06:00), resolution (3, 10:00, clears), afterglow (3, 22:00, clears) to the end of day 5 (`days: 6`, the lead's call). `need: ['calder']`: `eventDueDay` moves the start on to the first day Calder is on shift (day 10, a Wednesday, in a new game).
    - **Keys other owners read** (null = no change): `eventMod('wear', 'power'|'comms'|'chiller')` 1.5/2 at onset, 2/3/1.5 in the escalation; `eventMod('effect', 'flicker'|'blackouts'|'commsDown')`; `eventMod('routine', rule)` with rules doubleShift (dept:command, dept:helm), patrol (dept:watch), closed ('3|Bow lounge', 'type:callhome'), stayIn (all, share .4/.5), rush (callhome, 10:00-18:00 on resolution day); `eventMod('needs', 'sleep'|'social')` .8/1.2 in the escalation; `eventMod('galley', 'menu'|'cook'|'grab'|'trays')` stormStew/.75/1.5/6 in the escalation; `eventMod('jobs')` the phase's job list (id, title, tier, at, parts, bring, stops by placeId or room+types or machine kind on a deck). `news` carries seeds for section 20; `moments` the felt-everywhere beats (front: jolt, lightsDip, flicker; surge: jolt, lightsDip) and the party gathering (bow lounge 19:00, resolution day).
    - **Threads** (lower is better; who scores, the lead 2026-10-16): `blackout` minutes (section 17), `spoiled` meals and `bridgeHungry` hours (section 16), `calls` backlog hours (section 18 itself, `scores.resolution`, `stormCallBacklog`: the calls owed x 40 minutes over the booths, slower with comms worn).
    - **Texts:** `address.onset`, `address.resolution(c)` (by band), `mail.warning`, `mail.onset`, `debrief` by band plus `debrief.mine(c)`. Texts may be functions of `evCtx(e)`: `{ band, mine, threads, you }`.
    - **Meetings:** registered in `MEETINGS` when the event runs (`stormBriefing` day 0 08:15 Works bay, Calder plus 'works:2' cast as tech1/tech2; `allClear` day 3 10:00 observation room). Scripts: lines `{ who, text, pose?, to? }` and one `{ choice: [{ text, rel, job, reply }] }` each; `gist` may be a function.
- **Pure in time (0.34.8):** `eventNow(t)` looks at the running event, then `SHIP.eventLog`, then the next due one, so a replay of any day sees what play saw or will see. The galley's replays read `eventMod(kind, key, t)`.
- **Traditions in an event:** `eventPauses(ev, d)`: a tradition whose start or end falls in one of the event's `pause` phases doesn't happen, unless it's in `keep` (birthdays go on). In the storm: onset and escalation (no quiz, Friday tea, jam). From `eventNow`, so plans agree on every day.
- **At run time (0.34.9):**
    - **Meetings:** `meetingsTick(now)` once a ship minute: the `notify` toast 15 minutes before; `dialogueShow(script, m)` when you're in its room (`inMeeting(m)`: its deck, `where.room` on the room's name) while it's on (`len`, default 15); `dialogueClose()` when you walk out (the gist by Mail, quietly); the gist at the end if you never came. Flags in `SHIP.event.mine` (`told:`, `met:`, `gist:` + meeting id), so a reload doesn't replay it. `meetingCast(m)`: ROSTER ids, and '<dept>:N' -> N juniors of that department on shift that day, by hash (k 602), as tech1..techN.
    - **The panel:** `dialogueView()` -> `{ title, name, who, text, to, pose, choices, i, n }`; `dialogueStep(k)` goes on (k: the reply picked). Section 19's `deviceDialogue(view, onTap)` draws it when it exists (`deviceDialogue(null)` to close); until then `DIALOGUE_EL`, a strip at the bottom (92vw on phones). A choice: `eventMine('choice:<script>', k + 1)`, `relAdd(id, 'meeting:<id>', pts)` when section 20 has it, `eventTakeJob(job)` (claims section 17's posted job with `event` and `eventJob` matching, else `SHIP.event.mine['wants:<job>']` for section 17 to honour when it posts), and the reply goes in next.
    - **Felt everywhere:** `eventMomentsTick(from, to)` fires a moment's `feel` within 3 ship minutes of its time, in play only (`FEEL_DONE`); `eventFeel(kinds, len)` -> `shipFeel(kinds, len)` (the performance engineer's) or the stand-in in `eventFeelFrame()`, which runs every frame from `eventsTick` (after the lights and camera are set, before the render): a 0.7 s camera shake, lights to 40% and back over about 3 s, a rare flicker while `eventMod('effect', 'flicker')`. With no event it changes nothing (cadet_test identical).
- **Joined up (0.34.10):** `eventOn(d)` and `eventGatherings(d)` for section 14 (pure, any day); `newsAdd` (section 20) for each `news` item at its time, id `<news id>@<startDay>`, seed = `from` plus `seed`, lines evaluated with `evCtx`, life 2 days, flagged in `SHIP.event.mine['news:<id>']`; `eventsDress(now)` -> `dressShow(dressing for the phase + SHIP.traces)` once a ship minute; `deviceDialogue(view)` with `dialogueNext` / `dialogueChoose` / `dialogueClose` for section 19's taps. `eventModAt` is gone: `eventMod(kind, key, t)` already takes the minute.
- **Hooks for others (0.34.13):** `eventRoutines(t)` -> the rule list in force (section 14); `callOwed(p, startDay)` (the 'rush' rule and the backlog agree); `eventGuestDays(g)` -> `[arriveDay, leaveDay]` from the event's start day plus `g.arriveOffset` (else the event's `guestsArrive`, else its onset's day) and `g.stay`; `EV_SHIPWIDE` (gathering moments as traditions: `when: { ship, day }`, `venue` -> the architect's crowd spots from `'<deck>|event'` places with `gather` and no `lead`); `eventOneOff(o)` (`when: { date }`, k 341, saved without its functions, `SCHED_DATA` and later days' plans remade when one is added).
- **Occasions (C10, 0.34.11):** `OCCASIONS.<id> = { title, kind: 'summons'|'invite'|'meeting', by, deck, room (key), at, len, script, due(d), once?, notice? (days ahead), ask?, galley?, trace?, mail }`. `occasionsTick(now, quiet)` once a ship minute and on skips books what's `due` today (and `notice` days ahead), registers the meeting (event 'occasion', its flags on the record) and marks it `kept` or `missed` after its window. `occasionAdd(id, at)`, `occasionAsk(id, at)` (only when its person is on shift), `occasionDoorOpen(deck, room, t)` (from 10 minutes before to the end), `occasionMod(kind, key, t)` (what `eventMod` returns when no event is on: the table's galley batch on its day). Scripts: `readyRoomFirst`, `captainsTable`, `calderProgress`; `evCtx` adds `jobsDone` (your jobs on `SHIP.jobs`, which may be pruned: the device's Record should own a lasting count) and `commended`. The conference table waits for an event with a senior briefing (the diplomatic visit); officers' quarters invitations are section 20's.
- **Known:** the warning Mail goes at the warning's start (06:00), before Calder's 08:15 briefing; the debrief comes at the end of the afterglow.

## The answers pass (sections 14 and 10, the sim engineer, 0.26.8–0.26.9)

plans/answers-pass-plan.md, the sim engineer's rows.

- **Time (0.26.8).** `SHIP_RATE = 1080 / 1800`.
- **Feelings (16, 22).**
    - `crewMood(p, t)`, 0–100:
        - the needs averaged, with any under 40 counted twice;
        - plus `shipEffectsAt(deck, t).mood` (0 to −30) where they are;
        - plus 15 on their birthday;
        - plus up to ±6 of the day's own temper (`schedU`, k 500).
    - `crewFeeling(p, t)` gives a line or null, in this order:
        1. a birthday;
        2. the ship's worst trouble where they are, when its mood is −3 or lower ("Chief Holt grumbles that the showers run weak", from `MACHINE_FX` text);
        3. their lowest need under 30 (`FEEL_LOW`);
        4. a mood of 85 or more ("is in good spirits"), or under 40 ("seems a bit low").
    - `shortName(p)` gives the first name, or the title and surname for officers with a title ("Chief Holt").
    - `tapCrew` puts the line between the role and the bio.
    - Day 3 at 21:00: moods lowest 44, middle 78, highest 90.
- **Gatherings (17, 25; section 18's since 0.26.11).** `gatherings(p, d, F)` lists what a person goes to that day: `{ at, dur, where, tag, act?, walk? }`. `leisureUntil` stops a free-time block at `at` and takes the place, as the Wednesday drill does. People miss it if they're on the night shift that evening, at the gym, or gone to bed.
    - **Birthday** (`birthdaysOn(d)`, from `ROSTER[i].birthday`, day of the year `(d − 1) % 364 + 1`).
        - Who: the birthday person, 80% of their department, and 45% of everyone else.
        - When and where: the mess at 20:15, for 40–55 minutes (tag `party`, pose sit:talk).
        - With stand-in dates, about 24 people came.
        - Mess seats in the evening skip the Deck 3 cap (`take`'s `event` option).
    - **Observatory night** (`STARS_NIGHT`): Saturday 21:15, for 50–80 minutes.
        - Who: the stargazers and 30% of everyone else.
        - Where, in this order: places with `event: 'observatory-night'`, then the observatory's seats (the `observatory` sub-list now includes common-room), then any lounge seat or bench on Deck 2.
        - About 15 people.
    - Since 0.26.11 these two are gone from section 14. Section 18 owns them, in `SHIP_EVENTS`, with the same rules: `eventPlan(p, d)` feeds `gatherings`, and `eventPools(shared)` adds its place lists in `schedData`. The rules above are what section 18 copied.
- **Deck 1 (24).** Places with an `event` field are listed as `n|@event` in id order. Places only for an event (type `event`, or `event` 'handover' or 'walk', the bridge architect's: 6 handover spots by the ring, 5 walk stops) are kept out of everyday use (`EVENT_ONLY` in `schedData`); everyday seats an event also uses stay in it.
    - **The night watch:** Deck 1 night-shift crew spend the last 15 minutes of the shift at `@handover` (tag `handover`, act work).
