Side gains (`also`) come in from 15b's own frame loop (`ixSide`, from 0.37.1), following `A.done`, so section 15's task speed (`needEffects().task`) speeds them up or slows them down like the main gain. (0.37.0 used the activity's `until`, which section 15 runs at plain speed.)
- **What it leaves alone.** A tap section 15's `ACT_TAP` would take (treadmill, shower, mess table…), a real machine within section 15's `MACHINE_NEAR` (its `machineTap` runs it through section 17's `machineUse`), washers, places in another room than the thing tapped, private places (`privatePlace`: a room with someone else's bed), beds and bunks, notices, rotas, boards, screens, charts (`IX_SKIP`; garden beds are fine). Places crew hold (`heldBy`, `placeBusy`) say "In use".
- **Reach.** A place counts if its spot is within `IX_NEAR` (2.4 m) of the tapped thing's outline (its hit box), and the nearest to your tap point wins. A few seats have no tappable thing near them (two Deck 2 benches by the sample bench, one bow-lounge booth seat, one sofa end, one nutrient-tank garden place, one infirmary waiting bench); they're reached by tapping their neighbours.
- **The job board.** Tapping anything titled "Job board" (the Works bay board on Deck 5, and any later ones) walks you to it and opens a panel (`ixBoard`, made in 15b's DOM): your job first with Drop it, then section 17's `openJobs('you')` (from 0.37.2; before it, the device's rule), most urgent first, with Take it for the jobs section 15 can walk you through (machine jobs and chores on a machine, cart runs); chores without a machine (lamps, vents, deliveries, rounds) say "not yet" until someone builds their walk. Then the rest of the board, each with why it isn't yours (tier, crew only). Take it calls section 17's `claimJob`; the panel closes and the pointer appears.
- **Chores and turning in (0.37.3).** Chores without a machine (section 17's `CHORES`: lamp, vent, delivery, inspection round) are walked in 15b (`ixChoreTap`, before the place taps): the next stop is the first of `jobStops(myJob())` without `done`. A `fetch` stop is a tap in main storage (Deck 5, room `r`): 4 minutes, then `takePart` for what's needed and `jobStepDone`. Any other stop is a tap on something within reach of the stop's place (or in its room) on its deck: you stand at the place's spot (not the place itself, so a lamp over a busy lift queue is still yours) for the stop's minutes (`ixStop`, pose by tag), then `jobStepDone`. Walking away pauses; a stop only counts once its minutes are in. After the last stop section 17 does the work and sets `workDone`; the parts are used. The board's row for your job then says "done: turn it in" with Turn it in (`turnInJob`); chores are paid `JOB_PAY` there. With section 17's `TURN_IN` on, machine jobs and cart runs land in `workDone` too when section 15 finishes them, and complete at the board the same way (section 15 still pays them when it finishes them). Chores on a machine (calibrate, faults, filters) go through section 15's repair loop.
- **The marker and the progress bar (0.37.8).** `ixFrame` hides `#ixGoal` while `actProgress()` (section 15) returns something, i.e. while the progress bar is over you, so the two don't stack over your head.
- **The ship health panel (0.37.6).** A tap on anything titled "Ship health" walks you to it and opens `ixHealth` (`.ixpanel`, the job board's family): the first row of `shipHealthRows()` (the ship) then each system worst first, as a bar, number and word (`IX_STATE`: Good, Worn, Poor, Failing), then each system's worst machine when it isn't fine, then the jobs on the board. Redrawn each ship minute while open. Both panels close with Close, Escape, or a tap outside (a capture `pointerdown` that goes no further, so it doesn't walk you).
- **What you carry (0.37.5).** `ixHold()` returns `'carry'` (the crate) for a delivery chore between its pickup and drop stops, `'toolbox'` for any other job you hold whose work isn't done, else null. Section 15's `playerUpdate` asks it last when choosing `player.holding` (`... ? 'tray' : typeof ixHold === 'function' && ixHold() || false`), so a cart, parts in hand or a tray come first. The props are 8b's (the performance engineer's).
- **Tracking (0.37.4).** `SHIP.player.track`: `null` means the device's Jobs app has Track switched off, so `ixFrame` shows no job step (only an explicit `setGoal`, e.g. Maps' Go there); a job id or `undefined` means on. Taking a job at the board sets it to the job's id. Saved through `SAVE_EXTRA` as `player.track` and read back defensively. The device calls `clearGoal()` when Track is switched on, so the job step shows again.
- **First tips (Pillar 3, C13).** 15b says when; the device draws them through `tipShow(id, text)` (shown once each, saved there). Ids: `board` (within 3.5 m of a job board), `taken` (a job taken at the board), `machine` (within 2.5 m of your job's machine panel), `turnin` (work done). All inert until `tipShow` exists.
- **The goal pointer.** `setGoal({ deck, at: [i, j] | place: id | room: key, name or RegExp, label })` and `clearGoal()`, for any section (the Pillar 3 map will reuse it). With no goal set, your job's next step (`ixJobStep`): main storage for parts, the machine shop for a part storage is out of, the machine's panel to look it over or repair it; a cart job points at the cargo hold. Drawn in DOM (`#ixGoal`), no draw calls, hidden with no goal: a bobbing marker over the place when it's on screen, a round arrow at the edge of a frame clear of the HUD (top 130 px) and the toast (bottom 110 px) when it's off; on another deck it points at the lift and says "Take the lift to Deck N: …". It runs on its own `requestAnimationFrame`, a frame behind the camera (not visible).
- **interactions_check.py (0.37.0).** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 interactions_check.py BUILD.html` (about 6 minutes): fifteen things tapped on Decks 1, 3, 4 and 5, each starting an activity with a pose and raising its need; a bar with every stool held says "In use"; the treadmill stays section 15's; a notice reads as before; a seat in someone's cabin is left alone; walking away stops it; the board opens, lists, takes and drops a job; the pointer's job steps (storage, the lift, the machine), `setGoal` / `clearGoal`, the arrow off screen and the marker on; draw calls under 450; no console errors.

## The arrival (section 21, the onboarding engineer, 0.42.x)

Pillar 4, Part 1 (plans/pillar4-plan.md). Section 21 sits right after section 20 and, like it, waits for first use. **Wave 1a (0.42.0) is on paper only: nothing calls into it yet, and test URLs never start the intro.**

- **The state:** `SHIP.intro = { on, step, since, day, done: { stepId: shipMin }, missed: [stepId], settle: { itemId: shipMin }, skipped, ended, escort, flags }` (`since`: when the current step became yours), saved as `save.intro` (`introSave`/`introLoad`). Day 0 is the arrival Sunday, so its minutes are below 0 (`introAbs(day, min)`). `flags` holds the week's flags only until section 13's flag store exists.
- **The evening:** `INTRO_STEPS`, one entry per row of the plan's A0-A13 table: `{ id, day, at, who, mode: 'lead'|'wait'|'meet'|'free', where, meeting, checkpoint: { deck, tile, room }, rel: { id: pts }, flags, fallback: { wait | until, mail, flags, later } }`. `introAdvance(t, here)` moves it on: a step is done when it's due and you're in its checkpoint's room, at most `INTRO_REACH` (2) tiles away; otherwise, after the fallback's wait (counted from when the step became yours; only 5 minutes while you're missing steps, so the evening still ends by 20:00), its Mail goes out and it moves on to `later`. Two steps in a row never have checkpoints within reach of each other (intro_check). `INTRO_FLAGS` lists every flag the intro sets: `introBegin` clears them (section 13's store outlives a game), and `introEnd`, however the intro ends, leaves the device, Calder met and every week's gate set. The points add up to the social plan's end state (Torres 10, Tomas 10, Pell 5, Calder 3, Brennan 3). Checkpoint tiles are provisional until wave 2 walks them.
- **The week:** `INTRO_WEEK`, beats by the clock: `{ id, day, at, until, who, also, where: { deck, room } | 'seek', settle, meeting, job, appt, escort: 'lead'|'follow', flags, mail, invite, end }`. `week1.d2` ... `week1.d7` at 06:00 on their days, `week1.done` at Day 8 00:00; Tomas's `socialInvite({ trad: 'sowing', day: 6, from: 'tomas' })` on Friday at 18:00. Missing the bunk or dinner on Sunday: the Monday briefing hands you the device and you meet Calder there.
- **Settling in:** `SETTLE = [{ id, title, teacher, place, done(kind, data) }]`, 15 items; `settleNote(kind, data)` ticks them from the ship notes (see the hook register for who sends what). All done sends Calder's note.
- **For other sections:** `introOn()`, `introStep()`, `escortPlan(p, t)` (section 10's walker: `{ mode, to }` or null), `introBeats(id, d)` (section 14's `introPlan`: `[{ id, at, until, where }]`), `introEnd()`, `introSkip()`, `introArrival()` (Day 0, for section 18's "after arrival" rhythms), `introBegin(name)` (the name goes to section 15's `SHIP.player.name` through `playerNameSet`). Escort: `INTRO_ESCORT = 'torres-zeke'`; flag `escort.torres-zeke`.
- **Hash keys:** 700-749 (none used yet; 700 is set aside for your table at dinner).
- **intro_check.py:** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 intro_check.py BUILD.html` (about 5 minutes). Parts 0-7 run on paper; part 8 walks the arrival evening in the real game (New game arriving, the manifest, following Torres across Decks 5, 4 and 3, the meetings, draw calls). Part 9 (reviewer 1's case): with storage cleared and a plain `?debug` URL, a first launch opens at Day 0 17:40 on Deck 5 with the manifest up, and a Day 0 save reloads at its step (the name comes back once section 15 saves `SHIP.player.name`). The reserved jobs and the week in play come with wave 3.

## Social life (section 20, the social engineer, 0.36.x)

Pillar 3's workstreams C4 and C5 (plans/pillar3-plan.md, Part 3 and "How news spreads"). Section 20 sits after section 18. Everything in it waits for its first use (`socInit`), because ROSTER (section 9) comes later in the file. Nothing in it calls `R()`; every choice is `schedU` with k 600-619.

- **You and the crew:** `SHIP.rel[crewId] = { pts, tier, met, lastTalk, known: [] }`, made on first use. `relAdd(id, why, pts)` returns the tier after; `relTier(id)` gives 0-4 (`REL_NAMES`: stranger, acquaintance, colleague, friend, close friend at `REL_TIERS` 0/10/30/60/100). `REL_PTS` holds the plan's starting numbers (work 6, meal 3, chat 2, favour 8, news 1, appointment 3, birthday 3). Points never fall below the tier you've reached. Friends of your friends (one of your friends with affinity 40+ toward them) give 25% more.
- **Crew with each other:** `SHIP.aff`, an `Int8Array` with one number per pair of the 74 crew (2,701 pairs; `pairIdx(i, j)` in ROSTER order), -100 to 100; `affinity(a, b)` reads it (ids or people). Pairs start from `affStart`: partners (the roster's `partner`, 0.36.46) at 90; otherwise same department +12, same bunk room +18, same shift +3, +5 for each shared habit, +4 for each shared trait, -24 for clashing traits (`SOC_CLASH`; -12 before 0.36.32), plus seeded variety of ±8.
- **Each ship hour on paper (`socialHour`):** everyone awake and at a place (`planWhere` at the half hour) is grouped by room. In a room of more than four, each pair spends time together with chance 3 / (group - 1), so each person talks to about three. Every pair together drifts (0.36.32): +1 with chance 0.7, up to 90, but a pair under 60 only climbs while both have fewer friends at 60+ (`FR`) than their cap (`CAP`: 20c's `friendCap(p)`, 5 for chatty/sociable/warm/cheerful, 3 for shy/private/quiet, else 4; 0.36.33); clashing pairs -1 with chance 0.4, down to -60. A pair reaching 60 or falling to -40 becomes news, once. News passes between pairs who spent time together, with chance 0.2 × weight (1.5 times that for friends at 40+, 0.3 times for pairs at -20 or less). It's decided against the hour's start, so news never runs through a whole room in one hour.
- **The news model:** `NEWS` items `{ id, about, lines, weight, seed, born, kind, event, life }`; `SHIP.knows[id]` is a Set of crew ids. `newsAdd(item)` is for the events engineer too: its seed know it at once. `newsKnownBy(p, t)` gives the news someone knows, freshest and weightiest first. `playerHeard(id, from)` writes it to `SHIP.heard` (the Ship app's feed), and the first teller gets +1. Items are dropped after `life` days, with at most 40 on the books. Gossip is born at 07:00 for birthdays (`birthdaysOn`) and the day's ship traditions (`eventsOn`; "today" before 17:00, "tonight" after), at 09:00 for habit changes (`habitWeek`), and for a machine going faulty, failing or broken (section 17's hour record `MACH_HIST`; told first by up to three people on that deck, and only once someone is there to notice).
- **Running it:** `socialTo(t)` runs the hours not yet run, up to minute t. It's called by the main loop (`socialTick`, once a ship minute), by every time jump (`SIM_ADVANCE`) and by social_check. A clock earlier than `SHIP.social.hour` starts afresh, and so does `socialNew()` (New game). One big jump gives the same result as hour by hour.
- **Saving:** `save.social = { ids, hour, aff (base64), news (with `k`: hex bits of who knows), heard, fault, pairs, rel: { id: [pts, met, lastTalk, known, chatDay, mealDay, workDay, favDay, seekDay, arc, arcDay] }, mail (story Mail to come) }`. That's about 6.4 KB after two weeks. A save with a different crew list starts the social side afresh. `SAVE_V` is unchanged: older saves simply have no `social` part.
- **Chat cards (0.36.1):** `chatOpen(p)` (from section 10's `tapCrew`, instead of the old toast) shows a card: name, role, tier and one line. `chatLine(p, t)` picks, in order: a birthday; news they know that you haven't heard (`playerHeard` as you see it); how they feel (`crewFeeling`, shown as what you notice, in italics); something about where they are (`CHAT_NEAR`, by activity, with the room's name); a life detail for acquaintances and up (`CHAT_LIFE` by habit, `CHAT_DEPT`); or a greeting by tier (`CHAT_GREET`, 10 per tier). Choices are `schedU` per person and ship hour (k 610-623), so a second tap in the same hour says the same thing. Buttons: **Chat** (once a ship day, `rel.chatDay`: `playerChat` for your social, `relAdd` +2, the next thing on their mind; a grubby player gets a remark), **Ask about…** (News / Their work / Home; Home reveals the next `about` detail at or below your tier, else `life` lines from acquaintance up, into `known`), **Help?** (favours come next, on the systems engineer's `favourPost` API), **Meet up** (`deviceDo({ app: 'appointments', with })` once section 19 has it). The card closes when you walk off (see "Card reach") or they leave the deck (`chatTick`, from `socialTick`). Someone asleep gets a toast instead. The look is a plain panel like the toast; the device engineer's skin can take it over through `CHAT_SKIN.draw(el, view)`.
- **Favours (0.36.3):** `favourOf(p)` returns their open favour (`favoursOf`, offered or yours), or today's idea (`{ idea, title, ask, post }`, not yet posted), or null. Acquaintances and up have one with `FAVOUR_ODDS` by tier (0.2 to 0.45 a ship day, at most one a day each, `rel.favDay`). The two ideas: fetch something (`FAVOUR_THINGS`) they left where they spent yesterday's free time (a `pickup` stop at that place, `bring`), or swap the lamp at their work station (the `lamp` chore, `where` their work place type). On the card, **Help?** shows the ask with **I'll do it** (`favourPost` + `favourAccept`; it's in your Jobs, and section 15b's arrow follows its stops) or **Not now** (`favourDecline`). Once its stops are done (`workDone`), the button reads **Done: here you go**: `favourTurnIn`, then `relAdd` +8 and thanks. All of it checks that section 17's functions exist; without them, Help? says they need nothing. Tested on a scratch merge with 0.33.24; the full loop worked.
- **Ask about… topics (0.36.3):** Their work reads the roster's `about` items with topic `work`, else a department line. Home reads the other topics (home, past, hobby, secret), up to your tier (0-4), then `life` lines from acquaintance up.
- **Friends come and find you (0.36.4):** `socialSeek(m)`, once a ship minute. It picks a friend (tier 3+) on your deck within `SEEK_NEAR` (10) tiles whose `whereIs` act is `leisure` or `off` (not a tea break). With `SEEK_ODDS` (0.06) a minute, once a friend a ship day (`rel.seekDay`, saved), they wave you over: `#seek`, a small chip at the right edge ("Pell waves you over."). It's never a pop-up you must answer: it goes after `SEEK_SECS` (25 real seconds), when they move off, or when a card opens. Only one shows at a time, and none while a card is open or you're asleep. Tapping it turns you to them and opens their card with a greeting (`chatOpen(p, true)` → `chatGreet`). They don't walk over yet; that needs a hook in section 10's crew agents (the sim engineer).
- **Chatter (0.36.5):** `socialChatter(m)`, once a ship minute. With `CHATTER_ODDS` (0.35) a minute, and at most one line every `CHATTER_GAP` (12) real seconds, someone awake within `CHATTER_NEAR` (7) tiles and well inside the screen says a line through `bubbleSay(agent.h, text)` (section 8b; at most two on screen). The line is news they know that you haven't heard (`playerHeard(id, who, true)`: overheard, so no relationship nudge), else, 30% of the time, small talk by activity (`CHATTER_IDLE`: meal, work, free). The last 8 lines aren't repeated. `bubbles.hidden` is set while a chat card is open (the voice budget). Tested on a scratch merge with the performance engineer's c9d3884.
- **For the device's apps (0.36.6):** `crewContacts({ all })` returns everyone you've met (or all 74), closest first: `{ id, name, role, dept, tier, tierName, pts, toNext, met, lastTalk, known, where }`. `known` is the life details you've been told; `where` (`crewWhere`: `{ deck, room, act, placeId }`) is filled only for acquaintances and up, or anyone at work in a public department (`PUBLIC_WORK`: galley, infirmary, works), as the plan's Maps rule says. `newsFeed()` returns what you've heard, newest first: `{ id, line, from (a name or null), at, kind }`. Section 19 draws them.
- **The Crew app (0.36.8):** section 20 registers `deviceApp({ id: 'crew', ... })` at load when `deviceApp` exists (section 19 sits earlier in the file). The list comes from `crewContacts()`; tap a row (`act('show', id)`) for their tier, points to the next tier and what you know, with Where are they? (the device's Roster, 0.36.17) and Meet up; Back returns to the list (`CREW_PICK`). Icon `crew`, order 50 (0.36.9). When a tier goes up, `relAdd` calls `deviceNotify({ app: 'crew', text })` for a badge, not a toast (the card says it in person). `shipNews(limit)` returns `[{ text, from, at }]` for the Ship app. Tested on a scratch merge with the device engineer's claude/device head.
- **Seating by friendship (0.36.7, for section 14):** `affinityPlan(a, b, d)` is what plans may read. It's pure (`affStart`, cached), because days get planned early and re-planned after a reload, so live affinity would make plans differ (the old sim engineer's answer; a weekly snapshot isn't safe). The sim side still to do, in `take()` in schedBuild: for mess-seat and lounge-seat, first seats within about 2 tiles of someone already seated with affinityPlan >= 30, and skip seats next to anyone at <= -30. `occ` needs to record who holds a place (`[from, to, personId]`).
- **Story arcs (0.36.10):** `STORY_ARCS.<id>` is four beats `{ say, mail? }` for Tomas, Pell and Rina. Beat i is ready (`arcBeat`) when your tier is at least i + 1, beats are told in order (`rel.arc`), and at most one is told a ship day (`rel.arcDay`). `chatLine` puts a ready beat first (kind `story`); showing it on the card or after Chat calls `arcTold`, which queues the beat's Mail (`SHIP.social.mail`, sent by `socialMail` from `socialTick` once `after` ship minutes have passed; saved). Pell's show (0.36.14): a beat with `show` calls `eventOneOff({ ...show, day: tomorrow, host, from })`; if that works it stores `rel.showDay` (saved) and skips the beat's own Mail (the event sends the evening-before Mail). A beat with `afterShow` waits until the day after the show. `say` may be a function: Pell's last beat counts the crew whose plans went to the show (`showCrowd`) and whether you joined (`showJoined`, section 18's `SHIP.events.joined`). Still to come: Tomas's observatory invitation as an appointment (the device engineer's Appointments), and arcs for the officers.
- **Small dramas (0.36.11):** when a pair crosses into rivals (affinity -40, `socPairNews`), `SHIP.social.feuds['a:b'] = { until: +3 days, talked: [] }` (saved). Their card line is a grumble about the other (`feudOf`, kind `feud`) until you've chatted with them. The first one you chat with softens ("Maybe I was a bit hard on Rina."). Chat with the second and `feudTalk` makes it up: affinity back to -10, the pair marked 'm', a `madeup` news item, and `relAdd` +2 from each. An unsmoothed feud fades when it runs out.
- **Appointments (0.36.13):** `socialAppts()`, once a ship minute from `socialTick`, gives `REL_PTS.appt` to everyone in an appointment that turned `kept` (section 14's `apptTick`), once (`a.socKept`). For a `missed` one in the last three days, `apptMissedBy(p)` puts a remark first in `chatLine` (kind `missed`, `apptRemark`: the room and when), said once (`a.socSaid`); nothing is lost. Both flags ride on the appointment object in `SHIP.device.appts`, so the device's save keeps them.
- **Discourtesy (0.36.16, the diplomatic visit):** `socCourtesy(h, rooms)` runs in `socialHour` while `eventMod('effect', 'guests')` is on. `guestWarmth(p, t)` = crewMood, minus `COURTESY_PRICKLY` traits (suspicious 30; gruff, blunt, sarcastic 15), plus 15 if you're friends (tier 3+), plus 10 if you chatted today. At 20:00, Holt under 45 means `eventScore('discourtesy', 1)`. In any hour, a guest of the event (GUESTS with that `event`, if section 14 plans them) in a room with crew under 35 adds 1. Each counts at most once a ship day (`SHIP.social.courtesy`, saved). On a scratch merge with events 0.34.16: Holt alone gives 1 a day (4 over the visit; good 2, bad 6); a befriended Holt you chat with gives none. During the visit Holt's card line grumbles (kind `guests`). `crewWhere` returns null for people asleep or washing.
- **The query API (0.36.20, Pillar 4 A1; plans/social-deep-plan.md Part 6).** Pure functions the dialogue engine's line conditions read; none changes state except `memTold`. `mem(id, key)`: their heaviest memory of you whose key is `key` or starts with `key + '.'` (`{ key, day, w, told }`; `SHIP.mem[id] = [[key, day, w, told], ...]`, filled from A2). `memTold(id, key)` marks the heaviest untold match told. `warmth(id)`: `'warm'` when their memories weigh 40+ or you talked in the last 3 days, else `'cold'`. `affBand(a, b)`: `'rival'` (-40 or less), `'friend'` (60+), `'neutral'`. `bondOf(id, kind)`: the crewmate's id or null, from `SHIP.social.bonds['a:b'] = { kind, since }` (social B fills it; for `mentor`, a is the mentor and b the mentee, and `bondOf(x, 'mentee')` reads the same entries); `'couple'` also reads the roster's `partner`. `moodOf(p)`: crewMood under 40 `'low'`, 70+ `'high'`. `beatOf(arcId)`: beats told (`rel.arc`). `knowsNews(p, id)`: an exact news id, or a prefix before a `:` (`'trad'`, `'bday:tomas'`). `repBand(dept)` (`new` 0-19, `known` 20-39, `well` 40-69, `theirs` 70+; from `SHIP.rep.dept`, default 10 until A6) and `repShip(kind)` (`low` under 35, `high` 65+, default 50). `knownFor()`: `[]` until A6. `aboard(p, d)` is section 14's (the sim engineer), not section 20's. `cue(kind)`: a recent ship note, matched by prefix, `.you` on the end wanting one you did. `socialFill(p, name, ctx)` fills `{me}`, `{nick}` (the voice card's `words.nick[tier]`, else `cadet` below colleague and your name from colleague), `{partner}` `{friend}` `{best}` `{rival}` `{mentor}` `{mentee}` (through `bondOf`), `{boss}` (their department's chief or the captain), `{thing}` (`ctx.mem`: `fixed.<machine kind>` gives the machine's name, else `SOC_THING`) and `{trad}` (`ctx.trad`, else today's first tradition); null means skip the line.
- **Memories (0.36.21, A2; the plan's Part 2).** `SHIP.mem[id] = [[key, day, w, told, half], ...]`, at most `MEM_MAX` (8); a ninth pushes out the lightest (the oldest on a tie). `memAdd(id, key, { w, cls })`: the class comes from the key's longest known prefix (`MEM_KIND`), else small; a repeat of the same key refreshes it (today, the heavier weight). `MEM_CLASS` is [starting weight, half-life in days]: small 30/7 (chat, came, meeting, heard), medium 60/21 (fav, fixed, appt, visit, introduced, bday, mended, gift), big 90/60 (moment, show, helped), core 100/never (intro, keepsake), sour 40/4 (missed, grubby). `memFade()` runs in `socialHour` at 04:00; under `MEM_FORGET` (10) it's forgotten. A big memory gives up to three of their friends (affinity 40+) a small `heard.<key>`. Sources now: Chat (`chat`, or `grubby` when you need a wash; `bday` on their birthday), a favour handed in (`fav.lamp` / `fav.fetch` / `fav.job`), a kept appointment (`appt.<kind>`, with the +3) and a missed one (`missed.<apptId>`, `a.socMem`), a mended feud (`mended.<other>` both ways), Pell's last beat when you came to his show (`show.pell-show`); from the bus: `repair` you did (`fixed.<machine kind>` for up to six people who spent time in its room that day, closest to you first, schedU k 644), `tradition` with you in `came` (`came.<id>` for those there you've met), `choice` (`meeting.<script>.<k>`). Saved as `save.social.memKeys` (each key once) and `mem: { id: [[key index, day, w, told, half]] }`.
- **Lines through the dialogue engine (0.36.22, A3).** `socLine(p, kind, ctx)` asks section 22's `lineFor` (typeof-guarded, errors caught) and returns its `{ text, id, kind }` or null. `chatLine(p, t, peek)` keeps its order and asks the engine at each step, falling back to its own lists when it gets null, so without section 22 (or with a `lineFor` that finds nothing) every line is exactly as before (social_check part 7 proves it). The order now: missed appointment, the guests line (Holt), **hint** (20c's `momentReady(p, t)` -> a moment id: `MOMENTS[id].hint`, else the engine's `hint`, else a stock line; the card gains **Sit with them**, which calls `momentShow(id)`), story beat, feud, birthday, **callback** (when they have an untold memory, 60%, k 645), news, **cue** (when there are cues, 50%, k 646), feeling, **near** (ctx `act`, `room`, `placeId`) then `CHAT_NEAR`, **life** then `CHAT_LIFE` / `CHAT_DEPT`, **joy** or **about** (a bonded crewmate, 30% from acquaintance, k 647), **greet** (ctx `warmth`) then `CHAT_GREET`. Chatter asks for **smalltalk** (ctx `act`, `talk`: meal/work/free, `bubble`) before `CHATTER_IDLE`. ctx always has `t`, `tier`, `peek`: Chat looks ahead for "the next thing on their mind" with `peek: true`, then asks once more without it for the line it says, so the engine should note nothing (recently said, `memTold`) when `peek` is set. The hard-coded lists stay in section 20 as the fallback until the combined build shows the engine giving the same lines.
- **The first week (0.36.23, A4; the plan's Part 7).** `socIntroOn()` (0.36.34; section 21 owns `introOn`): false once the `week1.done` flag is set; else section 21's `introOn()` when it exists, else `SHIP.intro.on` and not `ended`, else an escort flag. `socGate(stage)` is true when the intro isn't on, else when the flag `week1.<stage>` (or `week1.done` for `'done'`) is set: favours (`favourOf`) need `'d2'`, friends waving you over `'d3'`, moments (20c's `momentReady`, also asked by chatLine) `'done'`; invitations (A7) will need `'d5'` and visits (A8) `'done'`. `escortId()`: `SHIP.intro.escort`, else the crew member with the flag `escort.<id>`. On the bus's `introStep { step, who }`: the escort becomes your mentor (`SHIP.social.mentor`, saved; `bondOf(escort, 'mentee')` is `'you'`, `{mentee}` your name), and the steps in `INTRO_MEM` (arrival, dinner, shadowShift) give core memories (`intro.<step>`) to `who`, the escort, and Calder for the dinner. While the intro is on, chatLine asks the engine for **week1** lines first (after the missed appointment and the guests line; ctx `escort` when it's your mentor), and the mentor waves you over once each evening (18:00-21:30, 0.25 a minute) whatever your tier. `socialInvite({ trad | kind, day, from, at, where, len })` pushes `{ id, with: [from], at, len, where, kind: 'invite', status: 'asked', by: from, trad, inv }` into the device's appointments (Accept / Decline there), posts `simNote('invite', { from, id, trad })` and a badge; for a tradition it's the host's own `ev-<trad>` block that day (its place and start, at most 60 minutes), so the host keeps their day; null if they aren't going, it's in the past, or they've asked already. Onboarding's Friday call: `socialInvite({ trad: 'sowing', day: 6, from: 'tomas' })`. The roster ids are `torres-zeke`, `halloran-emeka`, `calder`, `tomas`.
- **Gifts (0.36.24, A5; the plan's Part 4).** `SOC_GIFTS` (item name -> `{ tag, from: 'store' | 'garden' | 'made' }`; tags tea, sweet, star, plant, book, garden, made, music, game) and `GIFT_MAKES` (the machine shop's three: keyring, bookend, gear pendant, from scrap) are for the sections that sell, grow and make them: they call `giftAdd(item)` to put one in your hands (`SHIP.player.carry`, false when full). `giftOf(item)`, `giftInHands()`. The card shows **Give <item>** (or "Given this week", `rel.giftWeek` = week + 1, saved as the rel array's 13th slot); `giftGive(p, item)` -> `{ how, text }`: `giftTaste(p)` is the voice card's `loves` / `likes` when 20c's `VOICE` has them, else from habits (`GIFT_HABIT`) and department; loved +10 / big memory `gift.<tag>` / "Loves <tag>." in what you know, liked +6 / medium, else +2 / small (`GIFT_PTS`, `GIFT_MEM`), all doubled on their birthday; posts `simNote('gift', { to, item, tag, how })`; words from the engine's **gift** kind (ctx `how`, `item`, `tag`, `bday`), else stock lines (k 648). Crew gifts: `socCrewGift` at 19:00 each ship day (in `socialHour`), at most one a ship week across the ship (0.2 a day, k 649-651), from a warm friend (tier 3+), as a "Left you something" Mail (`GIFT_NOTES`), kept in `SHIP.social.given` (saved, the last 20). `giftKeepsake(p, what)` (for 20c's resolution moments): one per person, at most 8, a core `keepsake` memory; `keepsakes()` -> `[{ from, name, what, day }]` for the shelf and the device.
- **Reputation (0.36.25, A6; the plan's Part 8).** `SHIP.rep = { dept, ship: { work, kindness, spirit }, cnt, wk, day, streak, bands, notes, thanks }` (saved whole; null until something counts). Departments (`REP_DEPTS_ALL`: command, helm, comms, works, watch, survey, infirmary, galley, store, garden) start at 10 (Works too: a new arrival has no reputation yet, 0.36.43); `repFloor(dept)` is 5 per close friend there (at most 25), never gone below, and climbed back 1 a week. Ship standings start at 50 and each Sunday (`repWeek`, 04:00 on days that are multiples of 7) move 10% back toward 50. `repAdd(dept, n, { work, kindness, spirit }, counter)`. Sources: the bus's `repair` you did (`repDeptOf(m)`: the department most of day 1's workers in its room belong to, else `REP_SYS` by system, else Works; +3, +5 if `state`/`was` says failing or broken; Work +2/+3; counters `jobs`, `fix.<kind>`), `tradition` with you (+1 a week to the host's department, from the tradition's `from`; Spirit +3; `trad.<id>`), `jobAbandoned { machine, dept }` (-2, Work -3), `jobUnclaimed { machine, dept }` (-1, once a day per department), `shiftStart { late }` (20+ minutes: Work -2 and a `late` count; else Work +1), `moment { flavour: 'listen' }` (Kindness +2); in section 20: a favour handed in (+2 their department, Kindness +3), a gift (Kindness +2), a mended feud (+3), Chat (`chats`; Spirit +.5 outside Works, +2 on a birthday; `night` late in the evening), a kept appointment (Kindness +1), a missed one (Kindness -2, and -1 to the department of an officer in it), three favours declined in a row (Kindness -2; accepting one resets `streak`). Bands (`repBand`, `REP_NAMES`): a stranger you meet starts at `REP_WARM` points (4 where they know your name, 8 where you're well thought of; `relGet`), favours are 1.5 times as likely from 'well' up, `repAskedFor(dept)` for section 17's "asked for you" mark, a band change is a quiet badge on the device's `record` app, and reaching 'theirs' brings a thank-you Mail from their chief and a note to Calder (`repNotes()`). Each Sunday: Calder's note for two or more late starts that week, or "Good work this month" when Work is high (once in four weeks), and one piece of news about you (`rep:<day>`, about 'you', seeded to Works and colleagues; never told to you on the card, only overheard). `knownFor()` from `REP_TAGS` counters (reliable, kind, chatty, quiz regular, gym rat, always at the garden, the one who fixes ovens, good with newcomers, night owl). `repView()` -> `{ depts: [{ dept, band, name }], ship: { work, kindness, spirit } as phrases (REP_SHIP_WORDS), knownFor, notes }` for section 19's Reputation page.
- **Invitations (0.36.26, A7; the plan's Part 5).** `socInvites(h)` at 07:00 each ship day (in `socialHour`), once `socGate('d5')`: at most `INVITE_MAX` (2) a day, none on a day that already has one. Traditions first (`eventsOn(d)`, not birthdays): with odds `invite.odds` (section 18's optional `invite: { host, odds, line }` on a tradition) or `INVITE_ODDS` (.5), k 653, the person who asks is colleague-or-better and planned to go (`ev-<id>` block), the host (`invite.host`, else the tradition's `from`) first. Then personal ones from friends (tier 3+; `INVITE_PERSONAL` .12 a day, 1.5x when warm, k 655-656; once every `INVITE_GAP` (4) days, `rel.inviteDay`, the rel array's 14th slot): the kinds are the voice card's `invites`, else from habits (`INVITE_HABIT`), always with `dinner` last; the first kind whose test (`INVITE_WHERE`, by the room's name, or the dinner block) matches an evening block (17:00-22:00, leisure or dinner) of their plan that day gives the place and time. Both go through `socialInvite` (an asked appointment of kind `invite`; the host's plan already takes them there). `SHIP.social.invites` (saved, a week kept): `[{ day, from, trad, kind, appt, said, line }]`. `inviteFor(p)` puts it on their card (kind `invite`, after the birthday; words from the engine's **invite** kind with ctx `trad`, `kind`, else the tradition's `invite.line` or `ask`, else `INVITE_ASK`); shown once (`said`). `socInviteMail` (socialTick): not said by 15:00 and still unanswered -> a Mail from them. A kept invitation: the usual +3, a medium memory `came.<trad>` (or `appt.invite`), and +2 more for a tradition. Plans' blocks are in absolute ship minutes (`s.from`), not minutes of the day.
- **Bunk visits and notes (0.36.27, A8; the plan's Part 5).** `socialVisit(m)` once a ship minute (socialTick), once `socGate('done')`, from 19:00 to 21:30 (`VISIT_FROM`, `VISIT_TO`), not while a card or prompt is up or you're asleep, at most one a ship day (`SHIP.social.visitDay`, saved). Each minute with `VISIT_ODDS` (.02) when you're in your bunk room (`playerInBunk()`: your bed's room on its deck), else `NOTE_ODDS` (.006), k 658: a friend (tier 3+, `rel.visitDay` at least `VISIT_GAP` (5) days ago, the rel array's 15th slot; free now by `whereIs`; warm ones first, k 659). Home: the `#seek` chip says "<name> knocks at the door." (`SEEK.visit`, `VISIT`); tap it (`visitIn`) and section 10's `crewComeTo(p, yourBedPlaceId, onArrive, { until: 22:00 })` walks them in when it exists and they're on your deck, then the card opens (`chatOpen(p, false, line)`, `CHAT.visit`: it doesn't close when you're apart) with their moment's hint when 20c says one is ready, else the engine's **visit** line, else `VISIT_LINES` (k 661); +3, a medium `visit` memory, Kindness +1. Ignored for `SEEK_SECS`: a toast with a kind word (k 657). At 22:00 (`VISIT_END`) they say goodnight and the card closes. Out: a note (`SHIP.social.notes`, saved, the last 10; the engine's **note** kind, else `NOTE_LINES`, k 660) and a Mail "A note on your bunk"; `bunkNote()` -> `{ from, text }` (the oldest unread, marked read) for section 15's bunk tap. Close friends (tier 4, free at 20:00, .3 a day, k 662) can also invite you round to their quarters: a personal invitation of kind `quarters` at their bed's place for 45 minutes (section 14's `doorOpenFor` opens the door for an accepted invite).
- **Crew come and go (0.36.28, A9; the plan's Part 8b).** `SOC.crew` is the roster plus `ROSTER_RESERVE` (when section 9 has it), so `SHIP.aff`, memories and the save keep one shape (the save's crew hash covers the reserve; adding the reserve once resets the social side of older saves). `socAboard(p, d)` (0.36.38): between `arriveDay(p)` and `leaveDay(p)` (section 14's `crewArriving` / `crewLeaving` when they exist, else the roster's `aboard: [fromDay, toDay]`), and section 14's `aboard(p, d)` always. Off the ship: skipped in `socialHour`, never seeded with news, left out of `crewContacts`. `socTransfers(h)` at 07:00 (before invitations): 10 days before `toDay`, news `leaving:<id>` (kind `transfer`, the roster's `transferTo`, k 663); 7 days before a `fromDay` after day 1, news `arriving:<id>` (kind `arrival`, k 664); on `toDay`, `SHIP.social.moved` gets `{ id, day, tier, mem: their 3 heaviest memory keys, letters, next }` (saved), a farewell booked ten days ahead with section 18's `eventOneOff({ id: 'farewell-<id>', name, day: toDay, at: [840, 870] on a Sunday (rotation day; the sim's leaver boards at 15:00), else [1185, 1230] (gatherings only take leisure time; 19:45 had the best turnout on the traditions engineer's paper runs), venue: 'shuttle-bay', where: Deck 5 Shuttle bay, host: them, chance: .2, ... })` (the traditions engineer's shape, 0.34.24; planned like a tradition, friends likelier, its own tradition note after), and a "Sorry I missed you" Mail if they knew you and you hadn't said goodbye. Friends (tier 3+ when they left) write: after 14 days, twice more two weeks apart, then every 42 days (the engine's **letter** kind with ctx `mem`, else a line naming your heaviest memory). Goodbyes: `leavingSoon(p, d)` (days left, 0-10, or null); within 2 days, from acquaintance up, their card's first line (after a missed appointment) is kind `goodbye`: 20c's `goodbyeMoment(p, t)` -> `{ id, hint }` (with Sit with them), else `byeLine(p)` (the engine's **goodbye** kind, else built from their memories of you); said once (`rel.bye`, the rel array's 16th slot); they also wave you over that day. `newHere(p, d)`: their first week aboard; chatLine's ctx carries `newHere` and `leaving` for the engine. `movedOn()` -> `[{ id, name, role, day, to }]`; the Crew app lists them under "Moved on".
- **Lined up with the others (0.36.29).** The frozen line-condition vocabulary (plans/social-deep-plan.md on claude/social-design, Part 6) adds `inWeek1()` (= `socIntroOn()`), `leavingSoon(id | p, d)`, `newHere(id | p, d)`, `movedOff(id)` and `memThing(key)` (`{thing}`: `fixed.<kind>` -> the machine's name, else `SOC_THING` by prefix), all top-level. `CALM_TYPES` and `placeCalm(p)` belong to the places (the deck architect's 0.38.9; section 20 dropped its own in 0.36.35). Section 22 (the dialogue engineer) owns the five list lines `CHAT_GREET`, `CHAT_NEAR`, `CHAT_LIFE`, `CHAT_DEPT`, `CHATTER_IDLE` (they become `linesLegacy(...)` reads) and its own first line in `chatGreet`: section 20 doesn't edit them or the lines next to them. `socLine` asks the engine for the old lists' kinds (`SOC_LEGACY`: greet, near, life, smalltalk) only when the person has their own lines (`VOICE[id].lines`), so everyone else keeps the old picks; `socGreet(p, t, peek)` wraps `chatGreet`. Social B (20c): schedU keys A 644-671, B 672-699; `bondOf` asks `socBondOf(id, kind)` first and uses its answer unless it's undefined; `SHIP.social.bonds` (keys 'a:b' in ROSTER order for the symmetric kinds; for mentor a is the mentor) and `SHIP.social.affWeek` (a base64 string) are saved, loaded and reset by section 20, so 20c needs no save hooks; `rel.known` entries starting with '@' are 20c's and `crewContacts` leaves them out. Onboarding (section 21): its step ids `hello` and `bunk` give `intro.arrival`, `chief` gives `intro.dinner` (with Calder), `shadow`/`shadowShift` `intro.shadowShift`; the escort flag is `escort.<roster id>` (`escort.torres-zeke`); with no `SHIP.intro`, the intro counts as running while an escort flag (or your mentor) is set and `week1.done` isn't.
- **Bond kinds (0.36.30):** `SOC_BOND_KINDS` = friends, best, rivals, mentor, mentee, drifting, couple (one spelling for 20c, `bond:<kind>` conditions and `bondOf`; `BOND_ALIAS` maps friend, rival, partner). Placeholders (`BOND_FILL`): `{partner}` couple, `{friend}` friends, `{best}`, `{rival}` rivals, `{mentor}`, `{mentee}`. Section 21's `SHIP.intro`: `on`, `ended`, `skipped`, `escort`, and `done` as a map of step -> minute; `introOn()` reads `ended` and the `week1.done` flag. Load order: section 22's baked blocks (`VOICE`, `LINES`) and section 18 sit above section 20; `ROSTER_RESERVE` (section 9) is below, so it's only read lazily (`socInit`).
- **Ship notes (0.36.20):** when section 13's bus is there, section 20 listens (`onSim`) for `repair`, `tradition`, `eventPhase`, `favour`, `gift`, `harvest`, `lateMeal` and keeps each as a cue in `SHIP.social.cues` (`{ key, at, until, you, deck }`, at most 30, saved): `repair.<machine kind>` for 2 days, `tradition.<id>`, `event.<id>.<phase>` and the rest for 1 (`SOC_CUE_OF`). `you` is set when the note's `who` is `'you'` (or `you: true`, or `came` includes `'you'`). `relAdd` posts `simNote('tierUp', { id, tier })` when a tier goes up. schedU keys for Pillar 4's social side are 644-699: social A (section 20) takes 644-671 and social B (20c) 672-699 (proposed to the lead).
- **Card reach:** a card closes when you walk more than `max(4, the distance when it opened + 1.5)` tiles from them.
- **Time together (0.36.2):** `socialTogether(step)`, once a ship minute from `socialTick`, looks at what you're doing (`ACT` in its `do` phase). Eating (`eat`) counts crew settled at mess seats within `COMPANY_NEAR` (section 15's table rule). A job (`job`, `fetch`, `make`, `stow`) counts crew settled within 3 tiles whose `whereIs` says they're at work. After `SOC_TOGETHER` (10) ship minutes together, each gets `REL_PTS.meal` or `.work` once a ship day (`rel.mealDay`, `rel.workDay`, saved). Guests don't count.
- **Hooks outside section 20:** one line in the main loop (`socialTick()` after `eventsTick()`), one in New game (`socialNew()` after `playerNew()`), and one in section 10's `tapCrew` (`chatOpen`).
- **social_check.py (0.36.0).** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 social_check.py BUILD.html [DAYS]` (about 2 minutes). It runs 14 days hour by hour, twice, with a stand-in player (chats and dinners with Tomas, chats with Pell, hears Rina's news) and requires both runs to match. It also checks that two days in one jump equal the same two days hour by hour, the save's size and a save-and-load round trip, the affinity spread (friendlier on average, under 30% friends), news of each kind born and spreading, and Tomas a friend and Pell an acquaintance after two weeks.

## Social stories (20c, social engineer B, 0.39.x)

Pillar 4, the social plan's steps B1-B6 (`plans/social-deep-plan.md`). A marked sub-block `// 20c. SOCIAL STORIES` at the end of section 20 (before `// 6. CAMERA`), ending at `// ---- end of 20c ----`, so social engineer A's merges in section 20 never clash. Lazy like section 20 (ROSTER comes later in the file); nothing in it draws or calls `R()`. schedU keys **672-699** (A has 644-671 of the social range): 672 thread, 673-678 voice words, 681 introductions.

- **Voice cards (0.39.0):** `voiceOf(id)` returns a complete card `{ id, tpl, words, needs, openness, loves, likes, invites, joy, thread, keepsake, avoid, mentors, lines }`, cached per person. Each field comes from the first of: the writers' `VOICE[id]` (baked from `content/voices` by the dialogue engineer's generator), the crew designer's generated field on the roster entry (`p.tpl`, `p.loves`, ...), the template (`VOICE_TEMPLATES[tpl]`, else `VOICE_FALLBACK` here: ten templates' words, to be replaced by the writers), and a guess: the template from the first trait that names one (`VOICE_BY_TRAIT`; else `steady`), openness from traits (`VOICE_GUARDED`, `VOICE_OPEN`), loves and joy from habits (`VOICE_HABIT`), mentors from patient/kind/steady, and the thread: `'arc:<id>'` for story crew (`STORY_ARCS` or `p.story`), else a theme from traits and habits (`VOICE_THEME_BY`), else a seeded one of the twelve themes (`VOICE_THEMES`, key 672). **Frozen thread spellings** (agreed with the crew designer's generator, 0.16.12, whose roster `thread` field wins): `homesick`, `exams`, `family`, `stuck`, `notgood`, `lonely`, `loss`, `talent`, `burnout`, `leaving`, `rivalry`, `startover`, and `arc:<arcId>` for the story crew (the arc id may differ from the crew id: Torres is `arc:torres`). The writers' theme files use the same names (`content/themes/<theme>.arc`). Today all 74 have cards: 13 brisk, 11 shy, 10 playful, 9 warm, 9 dreamy, 8 dry, 6 earnest, 3 gruff, 3 chatty, 2 steady; 20 guarded, 40 normal, 14 open.
- **Voice tokens:** `voiceWord(id, token, t)` picks one of their words for `hi`, `bye`, `yes`, `thanks`, `wow`, `sigh` (keys 673-678, per person and ship hour), and `nick`: what they call you by tier (`words.nick[tier]`; null means 'cadet' as strangers, then your name). `voiceFill(id, text, t)` fills the tokens and `{me}` in a line: through section 22's `linesFill(text, p, { t })` when it exists (one filler for chat cards, overheard lines and moments; it reads the same cards), else here.
- **Openness:** `relGain(id, why, pts)`, called by section 20's `relAdd` after the friends-of-friends bonus: guarded × 0.8, open × 1.2 (`OPENNESS.pts`), not for onboarding's `intro:` points or moments, and only when the card's openness is written (the roster's `openness` or `VOICE[id].openness`; `card.opennessSet`), never when it's guessed from traits, so it changes nothing until the crew designer's fields arrive (reviewer 1, 0.39.1). Moments use `OPENNESS.moment` (guarded 1.5, open 0.75) when they come (B3).
- **When the writers' data arrives:** `voiceOf` caches cards against the `VOICE` and `VOICE_TEMPLATES` objects it saw (`voiceData()`, which treats a not-yet-declared one as none); when they change, the cards are made again.
- **Steps:** `STEP_GATES` (light: colleague, 3 days known; heartfelt or serious: friend, 7 days, 2 days since their last moment; resolution: close friend, 3 days since the last heartfelt one), `metDays(id, t)` (days since `rel.met`, -1 if never met) and `stepGate(id, tone, t, last)`; the moments engine (B3) passes `last`.
- **What you know:** `knowSections(id)` sorts `rel.known` into sections, no counts: an `about` text goes under its topic (Work, Home, Hobby, Past, Their story for `secret`; a topic with no section goes under Home), a `life` line under Home (Ask about tells them there), and an entry `'@<section>:<text>'` (for moments and gifts later: `story`, `loves`, or any topic) under that section; empty sections aren't shown. `knowBlocks(id)` turns it into the device's blocks (a `head` text per section, then a list). The Crew app's person page uses it.

- **Bonds (0.39.1):** `SHIP.social.bonds['a:b'] = { kind, since, peak, seen }`, saved by section 20 (from social A's 0.36.29). Kinds are section 20's frozen `BOND_KINDS` (`STORY_BONDS` here): `friends` (affinity `BOND_AT.friends` 60+), `best` (each person's mutual closest friend at 65+, at most one each), `rivals` (-40 or less, until it climbs past -25), `drifting` (friends whose affinity fell 15 below its peak; ends under 20, or friends again at 60), `mentor` (key `mentor:mentee`; same department, a rank or more apart by `RANK_ORDER`, the senior's voice card `mentors`, 40+; mentees juniors or officers, at most `BOND_MENTEES` 2 each, one mentor each; formed at 04:00, dropped under 25). `mentee` is never stored: it's the other end of a mentor bond. One bond a pair; other keys are in ROSTER order. `couple` comes with B6. `storyHour(h, pairs)` runs from section 20's `socialHour` after `socCourtesy`: it takes the weekly snapshot, marks bonded pairs who spent the hour together (`seen`), at 04:00 lowers by 1 the affinity of bonded friends not together for `BOND_APART` (4) days, then updates every pair (2,701, cheap) and the best friends and mentors. Every change posts `simNote('bond', { a, b, kind, was })` (kind null when a bond ends). `socBondOf(id, kind)` answers section 20's `bondOf`: the crewmate with that bond, the warmest if several, null for none, undefined for kinds it doesn't own (and for `mentee` / `couple` with none, so section 20 can answer: you as the escort's mentee); `friends` also matches `best`.
- **The tuning (social A's 0.36.32, the lines are in section 20):** clashing traits start 24 lower (was 12), clashing pairs cool at 0.4 an hour together, and other pairs warm at 0.7 up to 90, but a pair under 60 only climbs while both have fewer friends (60+) than `friendCap(p)`: 5 for chatty, sociable, warm or cheerful people, 3 for shy, private or quiet ones, else 4 (the designer's numbers). Measured hour by hour: day 14, 18 pairs at 60+ (best 9, friends 7, mentors 5) and 2 rivals; day 42, 89 (friends 63, best 19, mentors 11) and 12 rivals, levelling off. No drifting bond appears in 42 days (friends here meet daily); it will come with shift changes and transfers (A9).
- **Introductions:** `introOptions(p)` gives up to three people you know at colleague or better, as well as p, whom p hardly knows (affinity under `INTRO_UNDER` 20, not their rival), your closest first; `introDo(p, id)` returns p's line. If their traits clash: "We've met. We're… different." and nothing changes. Otherwise affinity +`INTRO_PTS` (15, to at least 20, so they aren't offered again), `memAdd` 'introduced.<other>' both ways, news (kind `introduced`) and `simNote('introduced', { a, b, you: true })`. The button is on social A's card (0.36.31).
- **The weekly snapshot:** `SHIP.social.affWeek = '<week>:<crew count>:<base64>;...'` (the week before and this one), taken by the first `storyHour` of each ship week (week = floor((day - 1) / 7); Day 0 is week -1), saved by section 20. Sparse since 0.39.3 (reviewer 1: the full table grew with the square of the crew): only pairs at `AFF_SNAP` (30) or more either way, as `[i, j, v]` bytes, which are seating's two lines (next to friends at 30+, away from people at -30 or less); any other pair reads 0. About 1.7 KB for both at day 21 (was 7.2 KB). `affPlanWeek(i, j, d)` gives the snapshot of week W-1 for a day in week W, or undefined: no day given, no snapshot (a new game's first weeks, a save from before 0.39.1, a crew list of another size: seating uses the starting affinity for up to two weeks after the reserve lands), or one still to be taken (a day planned more than a week ahead gets the starting affinity, then the snapshot once it exists; days are never planned that far ahead). `affinityPlan` (section 20) uses it before its own `affStart`. New game clears it (section 20's `socReset`). Agreed with the sim engineer. **Section 14's seating still calls `affinityPlan(p, q)` without the day, so it doesn't use the snapshot yet; asked of the sim engineer.**

- **Moments (0.39.3):** each person's thread (`voiceOf(id).thread`) has beats. A story arc (`arc:<id>`) uses section 20's `STORY_ARCS`: beat 1 is the card line, beats 2 on are moments (`momentOwnsBeat(id, n)`, which section 20's `arcBeat` asks). A theme has three beats: 1 light (colleague), 2 heartfelt (friend), 3 resolution (close friend) (`MOMENT_TONE`). Scripts: `momentDef('<crewId>:<beat>')` reads the writers' baked `MOMENTS[id]` (section 22's LINES block, from `content/arcs/<arcId>.arc`), else `MOMENTS_BUILTIN[id]` (Tomas, Pell, Rina beats 2-4, from their Pillar 3 arcs; `say: n` opens with `STORY_ARCS[who][n].say`), else for a theme `MOMENT_THEMES[theme][beat - 1]`; this block never declares `MOMENTS` or `MOMENT_THEMES` (the dialogue engineer's generator does). A script is `{ tone, tier?, metDays?, gapDays?, hint, crisis?, keepsake?, learn?, after: { mail: { after, subject, body } }?, lines: [{ who, text, pose } | { say: n } | { choice: [{ text, flavour, reply, warm? }] }] }`; texts get the speaker's voice tokens (`voiceFill`).
  - **Ready** (`momentReady(p, t, ctx)`, from section 20's `chatLine` once week 1 is done, and its bunk visits): the next beat has a script; your tier and days known pass the gate (`STEP_GATES`, or the script's own numbers); the gap since their last moment (for a resolution, since the last heartfelt one); not the same ship day as their last moment, nor an arc's last beat; at most one serious moment (heartfelt, serious or resolution) a ship day for you (`SHIP.social.stories.serious`); not during an event's onset unless `crisis: true`; and calm (`momentCalm`): off duty (leisure or off) at a place whose type is in section 15's `SIT_TYPES`, or visiting you (section 20's `VISIT.inside`). `ctx.anywhere` skips the calm test (tests). In a three-day run all 74 crew have calm times.
  - **Playing** (`momentShow(id)`, section 20's "Sit with them"): the script goes into `MEETING_SCRIPTS['moment:<id>']` with `onPick` and `onEnd`, and section 18's `dialogueShow` plays it. The reply whose flavour is the one they need (`voiceOf(id).needs`) may have a warmer answer (`warm`).
  - **After-effects** (`momentDone`, once a reply was picked, even if you walk off after): `relAdd(id, 'moment', MOMENT_PTS[tone] + MOMENT_FIT if it fits)` (light 3, heartfelt 5, resolution 6, fit +4; times `OPENNESS.moment` once openness is written), `memAdd` `moment.<thread>.<beat>.<flavour>` (core for a resolution), the script's `learn` into What you know (`@story:`), `simNote('moment', { id, who, beat, flavour, tone, fit })` (section 20 gives Kindness for listening), a resolution's keepsake (`giftKeepsake`), the script's Mail, and for an arc section 20's `arcTold(p, beat)` (its count, Mail and Pell's show; done here too if an older `arcTold` takes no beat). State in `SHIP.social.stories = { serious, m: { id: { b, at, hf } } }`, saved by section 20.

- **Goodbyes (0.39.4, B5):** section 20 (A9) knows who is leaving (`leavingSoon`) and leads their card with a goodbye from two days before, for acquaintances and up; it asks `goodbyeMoment(p, t)` -> `{ id: '<crewId>:bye', hint }` (null once said: `storyM(id).bye`). `momentDef` builds `'<id>:bye'` through `goodbyeDef(id)`: the writers' own `MOMENTS['<id>:bye']` when there is one, else lines from where they're going (`transferTo`) and when (`GOODBYE_WHEN`), up to two of their heaviest memories of you in words (section 20's `memThing`), a moment you shared (friends and up), a choice of four flavours (three below colleague: no `share`), and a close word by tier (a keepsake at close friend). It plays anywhere (no gates, not a serious moment). `goodbyeDone`: `relAdd(id, 'moment', GOODBYE_PTS 3 + MOMENT_FIT if it fits)`, `memAdd` `moment.bye.<flavour>` (core), `simNote('moment', { tone: 'goodbye' })`, `giftKeepsake` at close friend (the roster's or VOICE's `keepsake`).

- **Couples (0.39.5, B6):** the roster's `partner` (crew designer 0.16.13: four couples, never the player). `storyHour` makes each pair a `couple` bond from the first hour (any other bond between them goes) and keeps their affinity at `COUPLE_AFF` (90) or more, so they sit together once section 14 seats by the weekly snapshot; for the first two weeks social A's `affStart` gives partners 90 too. A couple is never best friends, rivals, mentor or drifting, and isn't faded. `socBondOf(id, 'couple')` gives the partner (section 20's `bondOf` takes `partner` as `couple`), so the writers' `{partner}` lines work. No storylines, nothing that involves you; free time together is the sim engineer's call.
- **The arcs' shape (0.39.5, agreed with the head writer and the dialogue engineer):** the baked `MOMENTS['<crewId>:<n>'] = { who, thread: 'arc:<arcId>', beat, tone, where?, when?, tier?, metDays?, gapDays?, duty?, crisis?, hint?, mem?, flag?, keepsake?, learn?, after?, lines }` and `MOMENT_THEMES[theme] = [{ beat, tone, needs?, ..., lines }]` (speaker `them` = whoever has the theme). Beats told so far are `rel.arc` for arcs (section 20 counts its card-line beats; 20c counts the rest) and `storyM(id).b` for themes; `momentNextId` takes the next beat that has a script, skipping gaps (up to six). An arc with `STORY_ARCS` card lines (Tomas, Pell, Rina) tells beat 1 on the card; a new story crew member's beat 1 is a moment. `when` is a condition object judged by section 22's `linesOk(when, p, { t })` (no checker: not ready). `where`: `calm` (the default: off duty at a seat, visiting you, or an appointment with them), `visit`, `appt`, `any` (played in place, wherever you tap them: Holt at the Envoy's door), or part of a room's name as in `DECKS[n].rooms` (Observatory, Works bay, Gym; off duty, or on duty with `duty: true`). A reply's `favour` done inside the scene (Holt's `holt.invite`) is set as a flag like `flag`; favours that become a job come with B4. A theme beat whose `needs` names a roster field the person lacks is skipped. Memories: `moment.<arc>.<word>` from the reply's `mem` or the beat's, else `moment.<arc>.<beat>.<flavour>`; flags: the beat's `flag` and the reply's, set with section 13's `flagSet` when the reply is picked. `warm` (a reply object) replaces the reply when its flavour is the one they need.

## Lines (section 22, the dialogue engineer, 0.43.x)
Pillar 4's dialogue engine and the writers' kit (plans/pillar4-plan.md Part 2; plans/social-deep-plan.md Parts 6 and 9). Writers fill plain text files in `content/` (the guide: `content/README.md`); `content_gen.py` bakes them into generated blocks in section 22; `lines_check.py` checks them.
- **Where:** section 22 sits after section 17's end and before `// 18. SHIP EVENTS`, so sections 18 and 20 can read the baked data at load (no temporal dead zone). Its code is lazy: it never touches ROSTER or SHIP at load.
- **Generated blocks** (never hand-edit; re-run `python3 content_gen.py`; a second run is byte-identical; `--check` exits 1 when stale): `// ---- BEGIN LINES` (`VOICE_TEMPLATES`, `VOICE`, `LINES`, `CONVOS`, `MOMENTS`, `MOMENT_THEMES`), `// ---- BEGIN MEETINGS` (`MEETINGS_BAKED`), `// ---- BEGIN TRADITIONS` (`const TRADITIONS = [ ... ]`, the only one in the build: one tradition a line, exactly what the traditions engineer's `plans/trad_parse.py` reads (`trad_parse.read_all`; the interface is theirs and frozen); texts `{ text, tone? }`; without that module and with .trad files present, content_gen stops). Shapes are in the README's "For the engineers". On a merge conflict inside a block, re-run the generator.
- **`lineFor(who, kind, ctx)`** → `{ text, id, kind, tone }` or null. Pool: the person's own lines (`VOICE[id].lines[kind]`, score 300), their template's (`VOICE_TEMPLATES[tpl]`, 200), the shared bank (`LINES[kind]`, 100), each +10 per condition; only lines whose conditions all hold (`linesOk`). Recently said: -15 if this person said it in an earlier hour (their last 6), -8 if someone else said it among the last 12 (`LINE_RECENT`, not saved). The top score wins; ties by weight `w`, picked with `schedU(p.seed, hour, 750)`, so a second tap in the same hour gives the same line. Placeholders and voice tokens by `linesFill` (a line that can't be filled is dropped before the pick). A `told:` line calls `memTold`. `ctx`: `{ t, other, room, thing, trad, machine, dish, deck, act, place, topic, mem, peek }`.
- **Conditions** (the frozen vocabulary, proposed to the social designer for wave 1; `COND_KEYS` in content_gen.py, value lists in lines_check.py): social ones read section 20's and 13's queries through `lineQ` (each `typeof`-guarded; a missing query fails the condition, a negated one holds); the engine's are `time weekday season place deck event trad dept rank trait habit act birthday pair topic`. Agreed with the social designer (2026-10-17) with additions: `week1` reads section 20's `inWeek1()`; `beat=arc.n` (exactly n, baked `beatIs`); `aff:<crewId>:<band>`; `leaving:soon|you` (`leavingSoon`, `ctx.goodbye`); `newhere` (`newHere`); `moved:<id>` (`movedOff`); `guide:true` (`flag('guide.active')`); `place:calm` reads section 20's `CALM_TYPES`; `{thing}` is `memThing(key)` of the line's `mem:`/`told:`. `odds:` uses `schedU` keys 770-797 (by line id).
- **`overheardFor(a, b, ctx)`** → `[{ who, text }]` (with `.id` and `.tone`) or null: `CONVOS` whose `pair` and conditions hold for speaker A (B as `ctx.other`), the most specific, picked with `schedU(a.seed ^ b.seed, hour, 751)`; `ctx.skip` lists ids not to repeat, `ctx.noSerious` leaves out `#serious` ones.
- **Overheard, played (0.43.12):** `linesTick()` every frame (from section 20's `socialTick`); `OVH` is the conversation playing. Constants `OVH_ODDS` .3 a ship minute, `OVH_GAP` 20 s, `OVH_STEP` 3.2 s a line, `OVH_PAIR` 2.5 tiles. The voice budget (`linesHush`: CHAT, DIALOGUE, sleeping; section 20's `CHATTER_AT`/`CHATTER_GAP` shared both ways) and the saved ring (`linesState`, `linesRing(day)`, `linesHeard(id, day, tone)`; `SHIP.lines` in save.lines; an entry dated after today, from an earlier game, doesn't count).
- **`linesLegacy(kind)`**: section 20's old lists in their old shapes from the lines marked `old` (`content/banks/old.lines`), so section 20's picking (and every check) is unchanged until it asks `lineFor`. **`linesMeetings(into)`**: merges `MEETINGS_BAKED` into `MEETING_SCRIPTS`, turning `{me}` texts into `c => ...c.you` (for section 18 to call once).
- **Voice tokens** `{hi} {bye} {yes} {thanks} {wow} {sigh}` come from the card's `words`, else the template's, else `LINE_WORDS`, picked with `schedU` keys 760-769; `{nick}` by tier (null: `{me}`).
- **schedU keys:** 750-799: 750 the pick, 751 overheard, 752 overheard's odds, 753 its pair order, 760-769 voice tokens, 770-797 `odds:`; free: 754-759, 798-799.
- **Hooks outside section 22** (for the hook register): section 20, five `const CHAT_* = linesLegacy(...)` lines and one in `chatGreet` (`typeof lineFor === 'function' && VOICE[p.id] ...`: a person with their own lines greets with them; it peeks when asked about another hour, so section 20's look-ahead never counts as said); and (0.43.12) `if (typeof linesTick === 'function') linesTick();` after `chatTick();` in `socialTick`. Section 13 (0.43.14): `if (typeof linesNew === 'function') linesNew();` in the New game handler, after `socialNew`. Section 22 also sets section 20's `CHATTER_AT` while a conversation plays. Proposed, not yet made: section 18, `linesMeetings(MEETING_SCRIPTS);` after `const MEETING_SCRIPTS = {};`.
- **lines_check.py:** `python3 lines_check.py [GAME.html] [--strict] [--quiet]` (no browser, a second). Errors: unreadable files, unknown tokens or condition values, lengths (card 90, bubble 60, beats/moments/meetings 140; a placeholder counts 9), unknown crew ids, the banned list, unearned `#serious`, stale baked blocks. Warnings: unknown names, duplicates, American spellings, habits the person lacks, reading ease under 60. Reports coverage per person against the plan's targets (`--strict` makes it an error: from wave 2) and the voice budget's rules and supply. The simulated day of the voice budget in a browser comes with overheard and reactions in wave 2.
| `if (typeof storyHour === 'function') storyHour(h, pairs);` | section 20, `socialHour`, after `socCourtesy(h, rooms);` | social engineer A |
| `if (typeof affPlanWeek === 'function') { const v = affPlanWeek(i, j, d); if (v !== undefined) return v; }` | section 20, `affinityPlan`, before the `AFF_PLAN` memo | social engineer A |
| `if (typeof momentOwnsBeat === 'function' && momentOwnsBeat(p.id, n)) return null;` | section 20, `arcBeat`, after `const n = ...` | social engineer A |
| `function arcTold(p, beat = arcBeat(p)) {` (was `arcTold(p)` with `const beat = arcBeat(p);`) | section 20 | social engineer A |
| `{ const sc = MEETING_SCRIPTS[D.id]; if (sc && typeof sc.onPick === 'function') sc.onPick(k, c); }` | section 18, `dialogueStep`, after `const c = l.choice[k]; if (!c) return;` | traditions engineer |
| `{ const sc = MEETING_SCRIPTS[D.id]; if (sc && typeof sc.onEnd === 'function') sc.onEnd(finished); }` | section 18, `dialogueEnd`, after `DIALOGUE = null;` | traditions engineer |

## Your device (section 19, the device engineer, 0.35.x)

Section `// 19. DEVICE` sits **before** section 15 (after 14), so sections 15 and 17 can register at load. It's the game's one menu (Pillar 3 plan, Part 1). Two layers:

- **Apps describe.** `deviceApp({ id, title, icon, order, badge, view, act })`. `view(ctx)` (ctx: `{ t, wide }`) returns a VIEW: `{ title, blocks }`, each block one of `{ kind: 'text', text, mark }`, `{ kind: 'list', rows: [{ text, sub, mark, action, button }] }`, `{ kind: 'bars', items: [{ label, value, mark, note, level }] }`, `{ kind: 'buttons', items: [{ label, action }] }`. An action is `{ app, do, arg }`; `do: 'open'` opens an app, anything else goes to that app's `act(verb, arg)`. Marks: `up`, `down`, `new`, `urgent`, `hot`, `big`. Apps never build DOM. `badge()` returns a number or null.
- **The skin draws.** `DEVICE_SKIN` (open, close, draw, notify, button, wide) is the only code that touches the device's DOM (`#device`, `#devBody`, `#devBtn`, `#devBack`). It draws the home screen's tile grid with badges.
- **Other calls:** `deviceShow(id)`, `deviceClose()`, `deviceRefresh()` (section 15 calls `device.refresh()` every ship minute), `deviceNotify({ app, text, urgent })` (a badge on the app until it's opened; a toast if urgent), `deviceOn('open' | 'close', fn)`. Errors from apps go to `DEV.errors` and the app shows "(this app failed to draw)".
- **The shim.** `device.addSection(id, title, render)` registers a legacy app drawn by `render(el)`; an id a native app already has is ignored (so section 17's `jobsSection` registration is a no-op). `device.open(id)`, `close()`, `show(id)` (`'status'` is home), `refresh()`, `isOpen`.
- **Apps at 0.35.4:** as below, plus Memos, Ship, Record, and the Crew and Appointments shells. **At 0.35.2:** home (time, where you're expected and what's next, today's traditions, what you're doing, carrying, job progress, ship notes), Schedule, Calendar, Needs, Jobs (tracks only), Mail, Wallet, Settings (screen colour, screen effects, tips).
- **Your day (0.35.2).** `devDay(d)`: breakfast and dinner (`MEALS`), the day shift (`DEV_SHIFT`, every day: the player has no rota of their own yet), the week's shape (`DEV_WEEK`: Monday's briefing, Wednesday's drill, Thursday's inspection and the Captain's piano) and the traditions. `devTraditions(a, b)` reads section 18's `upcomingTraditions(a, b)` when it exists, else `eventsOn(d)`.
- **Reminders (0.35.2).** `deviceTick()` runs from `deviceRefresh` once a ship minute (not while loading or asleep): each tradition in the next `ahead` minutes gets one `deviceNotify` on the Calendar; urgent (a toast) unless it's during the day shift, where only `per[kind] === true` ("Remind me") chirps; `per[kind] === false` ("Don't") is silent. A new morning puts a badge on the Calendar. `SHIP.device.calendarRemind = { on, ahead, morning, per }` is saved.
- **Tips (0.35.4).** `tipShow(id, text)` → false if tips are off or that id was ever shown (`SHIP.device.tipsSeen`, saved). One card at a time (`TIP_Q`, `tipDone()`), above the sheet when it's open. Section 15b decides when (`ixTip`).
- **Camera framing (0.35.4).** `deviceFrame(on)`; `deviceFrameStep` (its own `requestAnimationFrame`) eases `DEV.fr` 0 → 1 and moves the camera's `top` and `bottom` down by a third of the view, in whole pixels, so the player sits in the visible top third. At 0 it never touches the camera, so builds with the device closed render identically. Only for the phone sheet; the wide side panel needs none.
- **Dialogue and chat (0.35.5).** `deviceDialogue(view, onTap)` with section 18's view `{ title, name, who, text, to, pose, choices, i, n }` (null closes it). Section 18 runs the flow (advancing after 7 s, replies, closing when you walk out); this draws and calls `onTap()` / `onTap(k)`; the X calls `dialogueClose()`. `body.dlgon` while it shows: tips wait. `DEVICE_SKIN.chat` becomes section 20's `CHAT_SKIN.draw` once the page has loaded.
- **Maps (0.35.3).** The VIEW block `{ kind: 'map', grid, rooms: { key: { name, kind } }, you, pick, goal, action }`; kinds `room`, `hall`, `service` (still or spine rooms), `locked`, `void`, `lift`. The skin draws one `<rect>` per run of a room's tiles in a row and one `<path>` for all the walls, with the pattern `#devhatch` for locked rooms.
- **Jobs and Track (0.35.2).** The device no longer takes or drops jobs (the board does). Track sets `SHIP.player.track` to null (off) or the job's id; it starts undefined, which counts as tracked. Section 15b decides what the arrow shows from it.
- **The skin (0.35.1), the old-school screen.** CSS tokens on `.device` (`--crt-ink`, `--crt-dim`, `--crt-bg`, `--crt-hot`, `--crt-glow`, `--crt-scan`, `--crt-radius`, `--crt-font`, `--crt-size`, `--bezel`), with `data-ph="amber" | "green" | "cyan"` choosing the phosphor. The dim colours are the plan's, brightened just enough for 7 : 1 (amber `#d09142`, green `#42b567`, cyan `#4ab1a3`). The scan lines and vignette are one `.crt::after` overlay; the roll is `.crt::before` moved with a transform; both animations only with `.fx` and never under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `.wide` (wider than 720px and landscape) docks it on the left; otherwise it's a bottom sheet and `body.devsheet` lifts the toast above it. A capture listener closes the sheet on a tap on the ship (the tap doesn't walk you) and opens it on a swipe up from the bottom 28px.
- **Saved:** `SHIP.device = { settings: { phosphor, fx, tips } }` in `save.device` (SAVE_EXTRA), read defensively. `SAVE_V` stays 2 until memos and appointments arrive.
- **device_check.py (0.35.0):** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 device_check.py BUILD.html` (about a minute) at phone (390 × 844) and wide (1280 × 800) sizes: every app opens from its tile and draws without errors and keeps the look rules (15px+, 44px buttons, no blur, 7 : 1), Back and Close, mail and notify badges, the shim, the panel on screen, nothing added to the 3D scene, `deviceOn` once per open and close.

## Deck 2 (builds 0.9.x, the Deck 2 architect)

Built from plans/deck2-plan.html (Sketch 8) on branch `claude/deck2-command`, alongside the Deck 1 architect (0.8.x). Everything for Deck 2 sits in one block in section 4d, between Deck 3 and Deck 6: the `// ---- Deck 2 kit ----` (materials `D2`, new pieces added to `KIT` and `WALL_KIT` with `Object.assign`, nothing calls `R()` at load) and then `DECKS[2]`. No shared engine code was changed.

- **Turn 1, the shell (0.9.1).** The plan's data went in as it was, with two fixes:
    - The plan listed `v,23,9` as Watch office glass, but that edge is between the lobby and the science corridor (both halls), so it would have walled off a row of the lobby. Dropped. The Watch office's glass is `v,23,6` and `v,23,8` either side of its door `v,23,7`.
    - The plan's Watch office plate was on `v,23,4`, which faces into the Second Officer's cabin. The plate is on `v,23,5` (now plain wall) instead.
    - **Bedroom plates** are brass plates on the header above each door (`nameplates`, wall kit `brassPlate`), not the teal plates beside it: Vance's and the Envoy's corner cabins have no wall beside their doors, and a plate over the door can't be mistaken for the neighbour's. Names: Capt. Marrow, Cdr. Vance, Works Chief, Infirmary Chief, Dr. Sato, Second Officer, Envoy Saruth, Chief Holt, Visitors, Senior Helm, Comms Chief, Counsellor, Watch Officer, Survey Deputy, Visitors. The six new officers are placeholders, by role.
    - **Mood light:** `sky #e9e6ff`, `sun #fff0dc`, `hemi .94`, `sunI .96`: a touch cooler in the shade and softer in the sun.
    - About 220 draw calls by the lift (empty shell).
- **Turn 2, the bedrooms (0.9.2).**
    - **`d2Suite(room, doorKey, o)`** furnishes a bedroom from its door. Bedroom doors are `closed`, and `roomFrame` only finds `door` and `open` edges, so `d2Frame` takes the door key instead (same frame: u along the door wall, v into the room). Layouts are drawn with the door in the left half and mirrored otherwise. Three layouts: hull cabins 4 × 5 (bed under the windows, desk on the far wall, en-suite in the front corner, wardrobe and armchair by the door, bookcase and plant at the back), the 3 × 5 cabins by the lift (V, H), and inner 4 × 4 cabins (`inner`, with a desk or `armchairs`). Options: `fab` (headboard, throw and armchair), `rug`, `papers`, `kettle`, `model`, `swans`, `trophy`, and `bed` / `desk` tap text.
    - **Corner cabins** X (Vance) and E (Envoy Saruth) are placed by hand, clear of the rounded stern corner.
    - **Taps:** every bedroom has one or two `see` items (bed, desk, the Envoy's tea table): tap them through the cutaway and the text shows without walking, since the doors stay shut. That's how the player sees into the bedrooms.
    - **Cost:** all bedroom kit uses shared `D2` materials and the rooms are `NO_FADE`, so they merge with the rest of the deck: about 250 draw calls by the lift, 290–310 over the quarters with walls down, about 540 zoomed right out (Deck 3 is 520–560 at that zoom). No glow sprites in bedrooms; the lamp shades are emissive.
- **Turn 3, the Captain's quarters and the corridor (0.9.3).**
    - **Captain's quarters `C`** (room-local 5 × 12, stern windows on the west wall at x 0, door `v,7,10` at z 5.5): wide `suiteBed` (`wide: true`) at (2.3, 1.2) with the en-suite and wardrobe by it, `alcoveCurtain` across z 3.0 (x 0.15–3.35, open on the door side), telescope at (.7, 4); `diningTable` at (2.3, 6.4) along the room with `light: true` (the deck's one real lamp; `LAMP_SLOTS` is 2), window seat on the stern wall, sideboard by the door; study: `captainDesk` at (2.25, 9.4) facing the room, low `suiteShelf`s along the south and east walls (tall bookcases hid the study from the usual camera), a globe and an armchair by the windows.
    - **Corridor:** `brassSconce` on ten wall edges (two flanking the Captain's door), `portrait`s on `v,7,7`, `v,7,13`, `v,9,8`, `v,9,13` (tappable: Varga, Moro, Brenner, Marrow; the three paintings are canvases drawn at load, no `R()`), `consoleTable`s (snug) under the gallery's east portraits.
    - About 255 draw calls by the lift, 330 over the Captain's quarters with walls down.
- **Turn 4, science, the Watch office and the array (0.9.4).**
    - **Kit:** `chartTable` (its top is `D2.chart`, a star chart drawn at load from a fixed hash), `chartRack`, `labBench` (`scope`, `centrifuge`), `labIsland`, `sampleScanner`, `specimenShelf`, `brassTelescope`, `commsDesk`, `signalBoard` (`D2.wave`), `fumeHood`, `gloveBox`, `probeCradle`, `launchTube`, `signalRacks` (`D2.rack`), `mastBase`, `sensorDish`, `humJar`; wall kit `starChart`. Reused: `controlBoard`, `desk`, `locker`, `dispenser`, `crate`, `coldShelf`, `windowSeat`, `stool`, `beanbag`, `clocks`, `msgBoard`, `notices`, and the `washroom` template.

The feed's material and glow are made at dress time and breathe (`PULSES`). `light: true` adds the deck's second real lamp and tints it blue (`LAMPS[...].l.color`), so Deck 2 uses both `LAMP_SLOTS`. The humming sample in the specimen store pulses the same way.

Everything else `2-<what>-<n>`: `shower`, `basin`, `toilet` (en-suites, numbered in placing order), `wctoilet` and `wcbasin` (the public washroom), `desk`, `armchair`, `cushion`, `windowseat`, `beanbag`, `captable`, `command`, `survey`, `comms`, `watch`, `bench`, `liftwait`.

## The crew roster (builds 0.16.x, the crew designer)

Pillar 1, workstream 2. Everything is in section 9, after `CREW`, between the `BEGIN ROSTER` and `END ROSTER` markers, plus two lines below them.

- **`ROSTER`:** 74 of the 75 crew in the format of plans/pillar1-plan.md "The roster". With you, that's 1 Captain, 1 XO, 4 Chiefs, 6 Senior Officers, 16 Officers and 47 Juniors; 60 on days, 15 on nights. Each entry has `id`, `name`, `story` flag (and `player` on yours), `rank`, `dept`, `uniform`, `role`, `shift`, `rota.offDays`, `bed`, `work: { deck, place }`, `habits`, `traits`, `looks`, `bio`, `life` and `seed`.
    - `dept` is the job's department: command, helm, comms, works, watch, survey, infirmary or galley. `uniform` is the uniform it wears, one of `makeCharacter`'s five `DEPT` keys (helm and comms wear command red, the galley wears works gold). `rosterSpec(p)` turns an entry into what `makeCharacter` takes.

    - Days off: two in a row, staggered round the week within each department (20–22 people off on any day).

## How decks work (section 4)

Unchanged from DEV_NOTES_2 in outline:

- `SHIP` holds state that outlives a deck.
- 4a is the current deck: per-deck `let` tables, replaced by each build.
- 4b is the shared kit, made once at load.
- 4c is `buildDeck(def)` and `unloadDeck()`. A build reseeds the random generator with `def.seed`, and unloading frees everything made after the build's id watermark.
- 4d holds the deck definitions in `DECKS`.

**Deck definition fields:**

- **Required:** `num`, `name`, `seed`, `lift` (the car's centre in tile units), `map`, `doors`, `rooms`, `windows`, `closedDoors`, `hull` and `dress(d)`.
- **For the lift:** `blurb` (subtitle in the lift panel) and `arrive` (toast text on arrival).
- **Optional:**
    - `hazard` paints hazard bands at doorways onto steel floors.
    - `markings` gives keep-clear outlines: `[i, j, w, d]` in tiles.
    - `plates` and `sconces`, plus Deck 4's `cabins`.
    - `lockedRoom(r)` gives tap text for locked rooms.
- **Deck 5 also has:**
    - `sealed`: door key → room letter. Its `closedDoors` are built from the rooms' `tap` text.
    - `workLights`: wall lights listed in data and placed by its `dress`.

**Door types** in `doors`:

| Type | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `door` | Slides open for anyone nearby. |
| `closed` | Shut; tap text comes from `closedDoors`. |
| `hatch` | A shut service hatch with hazard leaves. Counts as closed everywhere. |
| `glass` | An inner window: wall-textured sill, pane and header, lowering with the cutaway like a wall. |
| `lift` | Walkable lift doors. Opens for anyone near, unless a ride holds it with `d.force`. |

Two types are generated, never listed: `open` (between two `hall` rooms) and `hull`. An edge between two solid (void) rooms doesn't exist, so a door listed there does nothing.

**Room flags:**

| Flag | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `name` | Shown in the HUD. |
| `floor` | Floor type (below). |
| `hall` | Hall rooms join each other with no wall. |
| `void` | A solid block with hatch texture. |
| `tap` | Text for sealed rooms. |
| `pad: [i, j, w, d]` | The shuttle pad marking. |
| `spine` | The floor draws nothing from the random sequence. |

**Floor types:** `warm`, `wood`, `tile`, `plate`, `corridor`, `lobby`, `carpet`, `mess`, `steel` (diamond plate), `haul` (plate with lanes), `bay` (darker plate), `grate`, `lift` (ribbed rubber), `deck` (the promenade's pale planks, with guide stripes and an inlaid light line along the walls, for hall rooms), `gym` (flecked rubber), `garden` (green pavers). `carpet` only suits hall rooms: it draws a guide stripe wherever the next tile isn't a hall.
